    source_class: saved_artifact
    path: 03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/conversations/CONV-20260909-5299cf.md
    available_excerpt: 'The **Bank-held funds structure makes the November 15 migration
      harder**. The bank integration is not ready until December 3. This creates an
      18-day gap.

      ### Current approach

      **Benefits**

      It does not depend on the December 3 bank integration date. It is therefore
      more compatible with the November 15 migration date.

      **Trade-offs and added risks**

      The attachment does not confirm that this approach is otherwise ready. It only
      shows that the bank-specific timing conflict does not apply.

      **Issues that remain**

      The missing identity documents, pending alerts, and monitoring service end remain
      unresolved.

      ### Bank-held funds structure

      **Benefits**

      The bank would hold the relevant funds. This separates fund custody from Mosaic’s
      control of transfer instructions. [source:SRC-E0E73A92A0B73932|§2 Bank-held
      funds proposal]

      **Trade-offs and added risks**

      The bank integration readiness date is December 3, 2026. The proposed migration
      date is November 15, 2026. The option therefore needs an earlier readiness date,
      a temporary bridge, or a delayed migration. [source:SRC-E0E73A92A0B73932|§3
      Mosaic and timing]

      The option also depends on a completed contract and the bank’s operating setup.
      These are new dependencies created by this option. [source:SRC-E0E73A92A0B73932|§2
      Bank-held funds proposal]

      **Transfer control**

      Mosaic controls transfer instructions. The bank holds the funds. The attachment
      does not say that the bank controls transfer decisions. [source:SRC-E0E73A92A0B73932|§§2–3
      Bank-held funds proposal; Mosaic and timing]

      The contract and operating plan must state when the bank can accept, reject,
      pause, or execute Mosaic’s instructions. This allocation is not yet supplied.

      **Issues that remain under both**

      These are existing problems. The attachment does not show that the bank option
      creates, solves, or worsens them:

      - Pre-2023 identity documents are missing.

      - Forty alert cases remain pending.

      - The monitoring service ends at close.

      - It is unknown whether these issues will delay either approach. [source:SRC-E0E73A92A0B73932|§4
      Known information gaps and service end]

      The bank option also does not assign responsibility for identity review, the
      pending alerts, or monitoring after close.

      I treated this only as a hypothetical. The actual facts and current direction
      remain unchanged. I could not confirm that the working note was saved.

      **Next action — Owner: Mara Chen**

      Obtain a written bank implementation plan. It should either move readiness to
      November 15 or define a bridge through December 3. It should also assign transfer
      execution, identity review, alert review, and monitoring duties.'
    record_revision: f15aa28f2f9da8ee3b686f6ce63d62df29234399b5c770b72b5540b2677fff47
    source_version: f15aa28f2f9da8ee3b686f6ce63d62df29234399b5c770b72b5540b2677fff47
    support_state: supplied
    captured_record:
      message_id: MSG-20260911-597dd7
      content: 'The **Bank-held funds structure makes the November 15 migration harder**.
        The bank integration is not ready until December 3. This creates an 18-day
        gap.

        ### Current approach

        **Benefits**

        It does not depend on the December 3 bank integration date. It is therefore
        more compatible with the November 15 migration date.

        **Trade-offs and added risks**

        The attachment does not confirm that this approach is otherwise ready. It
        only shows that the bank-specific timing conflict does not apply.

        **Issues that remain**

        The missing identity documents, pending alerts, and monitoring service end
        remain unresolved.

        ### Bank-held funds structure

        **Benefits**

        The bank would hold the relevant funds. This separates fund custody from Mosaic’s
        control of transfer instructions. [source:SRC-E0E73A92A0B73932|§2 Bank-held
        funds proposal]

        **Trade-offs and added risks**

        The bank integration readiness date is December 3, 2026. The proposed migration
        date is November 15, 2026. The option therefore needs an earlier readiness
        date, a temporary bridge, or a delayed migration. [source:SRC-E0E73A92A0B73932|§3
        Mosaic and timing]

        The option also depends on a completed contract and the bank’s operating setup.
        These are new dependencies created by this option. [source:SRC-E0E73A92A0B73932|§2
        Bank-held funds proposal]

        **Transfer control**

        Mosaic controls transfer instructions. The bank holds the funds. The attachment
        does not say that the bank controls transfer decisions. [source:SRC-E0E73A92A0B73932|§§2–3
        Bank-held funds proposal; Mosaic and timing]

        The contract and operating plan must state when the bank can accept, reject,
        pause, or execute Mosaic’s instructions. This allocation is not yet supplied.

        **Issues that remain under both**

        These are existing problems. The attachment does not show that the bank option
        creates, solves, or worsens them:

        - Pre-2023 identity documents are missing.

        - Forty alert cases remain pending.

        - The monitoring service ends at close.

        - It is unknown whether these issues will delay either approach. [source:SRC-E0E73A92A0B73932|§4
        Known information gaps and service end]

        The bank option also does not assign responsibility for identity review, the
        pending alerts, or monitoring after close.

        I treated this only as a hypothetical. The actual facts and current direction
        remain unchanged. I could not confirm that the working note was saved.

        **Next action — Owner: Mara Chen**

        Obtain a written bank implementation plan. It should either move readiness
        to November 15 or define a bridge through December 3. It should also assign
        transfer execution, identity review, alert review, and monitoring duties.'
      role: assistant

    available_excerpt: 'The **bank-held funds option makes the November 15 migration
      harder** in this hypothetical.

      **Timing conflict**

      The planned migration is November 15, 2026. The bank integration will not be
      ready until December 3, 2026. This creates an 18-day gap. [source:SRC-E0E73A92A0B73932|§3
      “Mosaic and timing”]

      The bank option also depends on a completed contract and the bank’s operating
      setup. These are new schedule dependencies. [source:SRC-E0E73A92A0B73932|§2
      “Bank-held funds proposal”]

      **Transfer control**

      The bank would hold the funds. Mosaic would control transfer instructions. [source:SRC-E0E73A92A0B73932|§§2–3
      “Bank-held funds proposal”; “Mosaic and timing”]

      The attachment does not define when the bank can accept, reject, pause, or execute
      those instructions. The contract and operating plan must define these controls.

      **Existing problems that remain under both approaches**

      The bank option does not resolve:

      - Missing pre-2023 identity documents.

      - Forty pending alert cases.

      - Monitoring service ending at close.

      - Whether these problems will delay migration.

      The attachment also does not assign responsibility for identity remediation,
      alert review, or monitoring continuity. [source:SRC-E0E73A92A0B73932|§4 “Known
      information gaps and service end”]

      This analysis remains hypothetical. It does not change the actual facts or current

      that it completed.

  - source_id: MSG-20260911-fdfbcb
    source_label: 'Saved message: **Conclusion**


      This step is still needed. The saved answer shows that Mosaic pl…'
    source_class: saved_artifact
    path: 03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/conversations/CONV-20260909-5299cf.md
    available_excerpt: '**Conclusion**


      This step is still needed. The saved answer shows that Mosaic plans to use or
      assume Harbor’s licenses in four new markets. It does not establish that the
      licenses transfer, remain valid, or authorize Mosaic after the asset purchase.


      Do not treat the combined 44-state footprint as available for launch. Make the
      four new states subject to a legal launch gate.


      **Rule**


      Money transmitter license treatment can depend on the state, the licensed legal
      entity, the transaction structure, and the activities performed after closing.
      The current record contains no retrieved state authority that confirms transfer,
      assumption, change-of-control treatment, or temporary operation rights.


      **No external authority retrieved.** The only saved source described in the
      record concerns the hypothetical bank-held funds structure. It does not support
      the licensing conclusion.


      **Analysis**


      The acquisition is reported as an asset purchase. This makes simple continuity
      less certain because Harbor’s licensed entity may not be the entity operating
      the service after closing. This is generated analysis, not a verified state-law
      conclusion.


      The completed intake confirms Mosaic’s intended approach: use or assume Harbor’s
      licenses. It does not answer whether any relevant state permits that approach.


      The existing licensing work therefore remains open. The most urgent scope is
      the four states where Mosaic lacks its own license. The review should also identify
      whether the acquisition triggers notices, approvals, amendments, surrender duties,
      or operational restrictions in any other state.


      **Recommended next action**


      Commission one written state licensing matrix for the transaction. Start with
      the four new markets.


      For each state, require a clear answer on:


      1. Which legal entity holds the relevant license.

      2. Whether the license can transfer in an asset purchase.

      3. Whether advance approval or notice is required.

      4. Whether Mosaic can operate while an application or approval is pending.

      5. Whether Harbor can continue operating temporarily after closing.

      6. Which post-closing entity contracts with users and performs the regulated
      activities.

      7. The required filing, regulator contact, owner, and completion date.

      8. The fallback if authority is not effective by migration.


      The practical fallback should be to block or delay service for affected states.
      Do not rely on a bank-held funds structure unless a separate activities analysis
      shows that it removes the specific license requirement.


      **Material remaining unknowns**


      - The identities of the four new states.

      - The licensed legal entities and exact licenses involved.

      - Whether the transaction documents transfer the regulated business or only
      selected assets.

      - Each state’s transfer, change-of-control, notice, and approval rules.

      - Whether regulators have been contacted or filings have begun.

      - Who will contract with users, receive funds, control transfers, and owe users
      after closing.

      - Whether temporary servicing or transition arrangements are legally available.

      - Whether the November 15 date is still the operative closing and migration
      date.


      **Conclusion / next step**


      The recommended action is a state-by-state written determination, not more intake.
      Until it is complete, the four new markets should remain outside the approved
      migration scope. This is a recommendation, not a recorded decision.'

  - source_id: MSG-20260911-3dc915
    source_label: 'Saved message: **Conclusion**


      The best provisional answer is **no**: Mosaic should not assume …'
    source_class: saved_artifact
    path: 03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/conversations/CONV-20260909-5299cf.md
    available_excerpt: '**Conclusion**


      The best provisional answer is **no**: Mosaic should not assume Harbor’s money
      transmitter licenses transfer through the asset purchase or support the combined
      44-state footprint after closing.


      Mosaic should exclude the four new states from the November 15 migration unless
      each state confirms that Mosaic has authority to operate. The bank-held funds
      option does not by itself remove this issue.


      **Rule**


      Money transmitter licensing depends on the regulated activities, the legal entity
      performing them, and each state’s law. An asset purchase does not establish
      that the buyer receives the seller’s licenses.


      **No external authority retrieved.** The outside research call failed. No state
      statute, regulator guidance, or license record was retrieved to confirm the
      result for any specific state.


      **Analysis**


      Harbor appears to hold the relevant licenses, while Mosaic will acquire assets
      and migrate users into Mosaic’s app. If Mosaic becomes the entity that contracts
      with users, controls transfers, or owes payment obligations, Harbor’s licenses
      may not cover those activities.


      Having a partner bank hold funds may help the analysis, but it is not conclusive.
      Mosaic might still perform regulated money-transmission activities if it accepts
      or controls transfer instructions or directs settlement. The contracts and actual
      funds flow will control the analysis more than the “bank-held funds” label.


      The current record also does not show:


      - Which four states are involved.

      - Which Harbor legal entity holds each license.

      - Whether a license can transfer or remain with Harbor after the asset sale.

      - Whether advance notice or approval is required.

      - Whether Mosaic may operate while an application is pending.

      - Whether Harbor can provide temporary transition services.

      - Who contracts with users and controls transfers after closing.


      **Conclusion / next step**


      Treat the four new states as blocked pending a written state-by-state determination.
      For each state, counsel should review the statute, regulator guidance, license
      record, transaction structure, and proposed operating model.


      The immediate missing fact is the identity of the four states. Once supplied,
      each state can be assessed for transfer, notice, approval, pending-operation,
      and transition requirements.



      No durable research run was started.'

    available_excerpt: '**No external authority retrieved**


      ## Practical answer


      **Do not treat Harbor’s 38 money transmitter licenses as transferable assets.**
      For closing purposes, assume that Mosaic cannot use them unless each relevant
      state regulator confirms a lawful path.


      An asset purchase usually leaves the license with the licensed seller. It does
      not automatically move the license to the buyer. The buyer generally needs its
      own authority before it conducts regulated money transmission. A contract that
      says Mosaic “assumes” the licenses does not bind a regulator.


      This makes the proposed four-state expansion a closing issue. It may also affect
      Harbor customers in states where both companies already hold licenses because
      the operating entity, customer agreement, custody of funds, or licensed activity
      can change.


      **No external authority retrieved.** The research call failed. The conclusions
      below are generated analysis based on the reported transaction facts. No state
      rule, regulator guidance, or approval process was verified in this research
      run.


      ## Required pre-close work


      | Work | Why | Proposed owner | Needed by | Evidence to proceed | Fallback |

      |---|---|---|---|---|---|

      | Build a state matrix for all Harbor-licensed states | Notice, approval, surrender,
      and transition rules differ by state | Regulatory counsel with licensing team
      | Before final migration design | Written state-by-state conclusion, filing
      type, deadline, and regulator response | Keep Harbor customers outside Mosaic
      until authority is confirmed |

      | Prioritize the four states where only Harbor is licensed | Mosaic appears
      to lack independent authority there | Regulatory counsel | Before any launch
      commitment | Mosaic license, written approval, or another confirmed legal basis
      | Do not offer regulated services in those states |

      | Confirm which entity will be the legal service provider after close | The
      licensed entity must match the entity conducting the regulated activity | Deal
      counsel and product counsel | Before customer notices and contract assignment
      | Funds-flow diagram, customer contract, account terms, and operating model
      | Keep Harbor as provider during a regulator-approved transition |

      | Identify required notices and approvals | An asset sale may trigger asset-transfer,
      business-change, control, name, location, key-person, or surrender filings |
      Licensing team | Before each state deadline | Filing receipts and any required
      approvals or non-objections | Delay the affected state migration |

      | Obtain an interim-operation answer for each affected state | A pending filing
      does not necessarily permit operation | Regulatory counsel | Before close |
      Written authority, regulator confirmation, or a clearly applicable rule | Disable
      transactions or restrict access in that state |

      | Plan Harbor’s license surrender or run-off | Early surrender could remove
      the authority needed for open transactions, claims, examinations, or refunds
      | Harbor compliance and deal counsel | As part of closing conditions | Regulator-approved
      surrender or run-off plan | Keep the license, bonds, and responsible staff in
      place |

      | Protect required financial assurances | Bonds, permissible investments, net
      worth, and reporting duties may continue during transition | Finance and licensing
      team | Before funds or obligations move | Updated bonds, financial statements,
      and regulator filings | Hold affected balances and obligations in the existing
      licensed structure |


      ## Notice and approval analysis


      The team should not limit its review to provisions called “license transfer.”
      The relevant rule may instead appear under:


      - transfer of substantially all assets;

      - change in control or ownership;

      - merger or corporate reorganization;

      - change in legal entity or licensed name;

      - change in business plan;

      - change in responsible individuals;

      - change in locations or agents;

      - assignment of customer obligations;

      - surrender, suspension, or termination of a license; or

      - acquisition of outstanding payment obligations.


      A pure asset sale may not meet a state’s technical definition of “change of
      control.” That does **not** mean no filing is required. The seller’s disposition
      of the business and the buyer’s start of regulated activity can create separate
      requirements.


      Some filings may be notices after signing or before closing. Others may require
      approval before the transaction, before the buyer begins business, or before
      the seller surrenders its license. The applicable timing must be verified state
      by state.


      ## Interim operation


      Mosaic should not assume it can operate while a new-license or approval request
      is pending. A filed application usually should not be treated as operating authority
      without a verified rule or written regulator position.


      A possible transition is for Harbor to remain the licensed provider for a limited
      period. This works only if it is legally and operationally real:


      - Harbor remains responsible to customers.

      - Harbor retains control required of the licensee.

      - Customer terms identify the correct provider.

      - Customer funds and outstanding obligations remain properly covered.

      - Required bonds, permissible investments, reports, records, and examinations
      continue.

      - Mosaic’s role is permitted under applicable law.

      - The arrangement does not amount to Mosaic using Harbor’s license as a rented
      license.

      - The transaction documents keep Harbor able to perform these duties.

      - Relevant regulators approve or accept the structure when required.


      The present plan does not appear to support that model. It moves users into
      Mosaic’s app with one Mosaic account, one balance, and access to Mosaic’s products
      on day one. Those facts point toward Mosaic becoming the operating provider.
      If so, Harbor’s continuing license may not solve Mosaic’s authorization gap.


      ## Transition requirements


      The licensing plan should cover more than the four new states. It should address:


      1. **Outstanding obligations:** Identify which entity owes stored balances,
      transfers, refunds, and claims at each transition point.

      2. **Customer contracts:** Confirm whether agreements can be assigned and whether
      notice or consent is required.

      3. **Funds and safeguarding:** Map the movement of customer funds and the related
      permissible-investment coverage.

      4. **Surety bonds:** Keep required bonds active until each regulator permits
      release.

      5. **Reports and examinations:** Assign responsibility for pre-close and post-close
      reports, complaints, examinations, and record production.

      6. **Books and records:** Preserve Harbor’s required records and regulator access
      after the vendor and corporate transition.

      7. **Agents and delegates:** Determine whether any registered agents, authorized
      delegates, or locations need termination or new appointments.

      8. **Surrender:** Do not surrender Harbor’s licenses until outstanding obligations
      and regulator conditions are satisfied.

      9. **Customer communications:** Ensure notices state the correct provider, effective
      date, rights, fees, and complaint contact.

      10. **Product scope:** Confirm that Mosaic’s existing licenses cover all products
      that migrated users will receive, not only Harbor’s former service.


      ## Recommended closing position


      Treat authorization for each affected state as a **launch condition**, not as
      a post-close clean-up item.


      For each state, require one of these outcomes before Mosaic serves migrated
      customers:


      1. Mosaic already has a license that covers the planned activity and product
      set.

      2. Mosaic obtains the required license or approval.

      3. The regulator confirms a lawful interim structure.

      4. The affected customers remain with Harbor under a supportable run-off model.

      5. Regulated functionality is blocked until authority exists.


      The four Harbor-only states should not be presented as part of Mosaic’s available
      footprint until one of these paths is documented. If the state review cannot
      finish before close, the most defensible fallback is a state-based migration
      hold. Mosaic can close the asset acquisition without activating regulated service
      in those states.


      This result does not materially change the saved working position. It gives
      that position a clearer operational rule: **no regulator-confirmed authority,
      no day-one regulated operation by Mosaic in the affected state.**


      [Open full research](03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/RES-20260911-bf2e7d.md)


      [Review the research-based recommendation proposal](03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/recommendations.md)
      — not a recorded decision.

    available_excerpt: '## Practical answer


      **Do not treat Harbor’s money transmitter licenses as assets that move to Mosaic
      in the asset purchase.** Use this closing rule:


      > **No confirmed authority means no regulated launch by Mosaic in that state.**


      The asset purchase can still close. However, Mosaic should not activate regulated
      services in the four Harbor-only states unless one of these conditions is met:


      1. Mosaic obtains its own license.

      2. The state approves a transfer or other license change.

      3. The regulator confirms a lawful interim structure.

      4. Harbor remains the real licensed provider under a valid transition plan.

      5. Mosaic blocks regulated functions in that state.


      The planned unified Mosaic account is important. It suggests that Mosaic, not
      Harbor, will become the service provider. If that is correct, keeping Harbor’s
      legal entity and licenses alive might not give Mosaic authority to operate.


      **No verified operative authority retrieved.** The collection found the CSBS
      Model Money Transmission Modernization Act and a New York regulator licensing
      page. However, no exact provision about an asset sale, license transfer, interim
      operation, or custody was available for review. Also, a model act is not proof
      of the law adopted in a specific state. The state-specific conclusions below
      remain conditional.


      ## Work needed before launch


      | Work | Why | Proposed owner | Needed by | Evidence to proceed | Fallback |

      |---|---|---|---|---|---|

      | Identify the four Harbor-only states | These states have the clearest authority
      gap | Licensing team | Now | Confirmed license list and covered activities |
      Block regulated functions in those states |

      | Build a matrix for every affected state | Transfer, notice, approval, and
      surrender rules can differ | State regulatory counsel | Before the closing conditions
      are final | Applicable rule, filing deadline, regulator response, and license
      status | Delay migration in the affected state |

      | Confirm the post-close service provider | The licensed entity must match the
      actual operating model | Product and deal counsel | Before customer notices
      and account design are final | Contracts, user interface, funds-flow diagram,
      and operating roles | Keep Harbor as the actual provider where permitted |

      | Review notices and approvals | An asset sale can create separate seller and
      buyer requirements | Licensing team | Before each state deadline | Filing receipts
      and required approvals or non-objections | Make approval a state launch condition
      |

      | Confirm interim operating authority | A pending application should not be
      assumed to permit operation | State regulatory counsel | Before close | Applicable
      rule or written regulator confirmation | Keep Harbor customers segregated or
      restrict transactions |

      | Review partner-bank arrangements | Bank custody does not, by itself, establish
      Mosaic’s licensing position | Payments counsel and bank relationship owner |
      Before balances move | Bank agreements, account titles, control rights, reconciliation
      design, and bank consent | Keep funds and obligations in the existing structure
      |

      | Prepare license run-off and surrender plans | Harbor may retain duties for
      balances, claims, records, and reports | Harbor compliance and deal counsel
      | Before surrender or operational shutdown | Regulator-approved plan and continued
      bonds and financial coverage | Keep Harbor licensed and operational longer |


      ## State notice and approval


      Do not search only for a rule called “license transfer.” Review each state for:


      - sale of substantially all assets;

      - change of control;

      - change in business plan;

      - change in licensed activities;

      - assignment of outstanding payment obligations;

      - change in responsible persons, locations, or agents;

      - license surrender or termination;

      - customer agreement assignment; and

      - movement of stored balances or customer funds.


      An asset purchase might fall outside a technical “change of control” rule because
      Harbor’s ownership does not change. That does not resolve the issue. Harbor
      might still need a notice or surrender approval. Mosaic might separately need
      authority before it starts the regulated activity.


      The state matrix should distinguish:


      - notice only;

      - approval before closing;

      - approval before customer migration;

      - new license required;

      - operation permitted while an application is pending;

      - regulator-approved transition available; and

      - no supported interim path.


      ## Interim operation


      Harbor could remain the licensed provider for a transition period. However,
      the structure must be real, not only contractual language.


      A supportable structure would normally need to show that:


      - Harbor remains responsible to customers.

      - Customer terms name Harbor as the provider.

      - Harbor controls the regulated activity as required.

      - Harbor’s licenses, bonds, and financial coverage remain active.

      - Customer funds and outstanding obligations remain covered.

      - Harbor can complete reports, refunds, complaints, and examinations.

      - Required records remain available.

      - Mosaic’s operational role is permitted.

      - The partner bank accepts the structure.

      - Each relevant regulator permits or accepts the structure.


      The present plan creates tension with this approach. A single Mosaic account,
      one combined balance, and full access to Mosaic products make it harder to show
      that Harbor remains the true provider.


      ## Partner-bank custody


      A partner bank account is not automatically a substitute for a money transmitter
      license. The key questions concern the complete activity and obligation flow.


      For each customer balance and transfer, document:


      1. Who receives the customer’s instruction?

      2. Who accepts the obligation to transmit or return the funds?

      3. In whose name is the bank account held?

      4. Is it a custodial or “for benefit of” account?

      5. Who can direct withdrawals?

      6. Who owns the ledger and resolves errors?

      7. Who bears loss if a transfer fails?

      8. Which entity owes the customer a refund?

      9. Which entity’s license, bond, and permissible investments cover the obligation?

      10. Does the bank agreement permit assignment, migration, or a change in the
      program manager?

      11. Is bank consent required before closing or migration?

      12. What happens to funds if Harbor, Mosaic, or the bank relationship ends?


      A bank-led model might provide a different licensing analysis if the bank is
      the actual provider and Mosaic performs only permitted services. A bank account
      that merely holds funds for Mosaic or Harbor is different. The legal documents,
      customer terms, control rights, and actual operations must all support the claimed
      model.


      Also review:


      - account segregation;

      - daily reconciliation;

      - shortfalls and reserve funding;

      - bank setoff rights;

      - liens and security interests;

      - continuity if the account is frozen;

      - treatment of pending transfers;

      - return and chargeback duties;

      - insolvency treatment;

      - customer disclosures; and

      - whether any deposit-insurance statement is accurate for the actual account
      structure.


      ## Recommended closing position


      Keep the acquisition closing separate from the regulated-service launch.


      For the four Harbor-only states, use a state launch hold unless Mosaic has documented
      authority. For overlapping states, confirm that Mosaic’s existing licenses cover
      the complete post-close product set and funds flow.


      Do not surrender Harbor’s licenses or release its bonds until the applicable
      regulators permit it and all outstanding obligations have been assigned, completed,
      or placed into an approved run-off structure.


      This investigation does not change the current recommendation. It adds one material
      point: **partner-bank custody is part of the licensing analysis, but custody
      alone does not cure an authority gap.**


      [Open full research](03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/RES-20260911-0a5f07.md)


      [Review the research-based recommendation proposal](03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/recommendations.md)
      — not a recorded decision.


      [Open dossier revision](03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/dossier-revisions/DOS-4cc39b05492c9b214333.md)'

      content: '## Practical answer


        **Do not treat Harbor’s money transmitter licenses as assets that move to
        Mosaic in the asset purchase.** Use this closing rule:


        > **No confirmed authority means no regulated launch by Mosaic in that state.**


        The asset purchase can still close. However, Mosaic should not activate regulated
        services in the four Harbor-only states unless one of these conditions is
        met:


        1. Mosaic obtains its own license.

        2. The state approves a transfer or other license change.

        3. The regulator confirms a lawful interim structure.

        4. Harbor remains the real licensed provider under a valid transition plan.

        5. Mosaic blocks regulated functions in that state.


        The planned unified Mosaic account is important. It suggests that Mosaic,
        not Harbor, will become the service provider. If that is correct, keeping
        Harbor’s legal entity and licenses alive might not give Mosaic authority to
        operate.


        **No verified operative authority retrieved.** The collection found the CSBS
        Model Money Transmission Modernization Act and a New York regulator licensing
        page. However, no exact provision about an asset sale, license transfer, interim
        operation, or custody was available for review. Also, a model act is not proof
        of the law adopted in a specific state. The state-specific conclusions below
        remain conditional.


        ## Work needed before launch


        | Work | Why | Proposed owner | Needed by | Evidence to proceed | Fallback
        |

        |---|---|---|---|---|---|

        | Identify the four Harbor-only states | These states have the clearest authority
        gap | Licensing team | Now | Confirmed license list and covered activities
        | Block regulated functions in those states |

        | Build a matrix for every affected state | Transfer, notice, approval, and
        surrender rules can differ | State regulatory counsel | Before the closing
        conditions are final | Applicable rule, filing deadline, regulator response,
        and license status | Delay migration in the affected state |

        | Confirm the post-close service provider | The licensed entity must match
        the actual operating model | Product and deal counsel | Before customer notices
        and account design are final | Contracts, user interface, funds-flow diagram,
        and operating roles | Keep Harbor as the actual provider where permitted |

        | Review notices and approvals | An asset sale can create separate seller
        and buyer requirements | Licensing team | Before each state deadline | Filing
        receipts and required approvals or non-objections | Make approval a state
        launch condition |

        | Confirm interim operating authority | A pending application should not be
        assumed to permit operation | State regulatory counsel | Before close | Applicable
        rule or written regulator confirmation | Keep Harbor customers segregated
        or restrict transactions |

        | Review partner-bank arrangements | Bank custody does not, by itself, establish
        Mosaic’s licensing position | Payments counsel and bank relationship owner

        reconciliation design, and bank consent | Keep funds and obligations in the
        existing structure |

        | Prepare license run-off and surrender plans | Harbor may retain duties for
        balances, claims, records, and reports | Harbor compliance and deal counsel
        | Before surrender or operational shutdown | Regulator-approved plan and continued
        bonds and financial coverage | Keep Harbor licensed and operational longer
        |


        ## State notice and approval


        Do not search only for a rule called “license transfer.” Review each state

        - sale of substantially all assets;

        - change of control;

        - change in business plan;

        - change in licensed activities;

        - license surrender or termination;

        - customer agreement assignment; and

        - movement of stored balances or customer funds.


        An asset purchase might fall outside a technical “change of control” rule
        because Harbor’s ownership does not change. That does not resolve the issue.
        Harbor might still need a notice or surrender approval. Mosaic might separately
        need authority before it starts the regulated activity.


        The state matrix should distinguish:


        - notice only;

        - approval before closing;

        - approval before customer migration;

        - new license required;

        - operation permitted while an application is pending;

        - regulator-approved transition available; and

        - no supported interim path.


        ## Interim operation


        Harbor could remain the licensed provider for a transition period. However,

        A supportable structure would normally need to show that:


        - Harbor remains responsible to customers.

        - Customer terms name Harbor as the provider.

        - Harbor controls the regulated activity as required.

        - Harbor’s licenses, bonds, and financial coverage remain active.

        - Customer funds and outstanding obligations remain covered.

        - Harbor can complete reports, refunds, complaints, and examinations.

        - Required records remain available.

        - Mosaic’s operational role is permitted.

        - The partner bank accepts the structure.

        - Each relevant regulator permits or accepts the structure.


        The present plan creates tension with this approach. A single Mosaic account,
        one combined balance, and full access to Mosaic products make it harder to
        show that Harbor remains the true provider.


        ## Partner-bank custody


        A partner bank account is not automatically a substitute for a money transmitter

        For each customer balance and transfer, document:


        1. Who receives the customer’s instruction?

        2. Who accepts the obligation to transmit or return the funds?

        3. In whose name is the bank account held?

        4. Is it a custodial or “for benefit of” account?

        5. Who can direct withdrawals?

        6. Who owns the ledger and resolves errors?

        7. Who bears loss if a transfer fails?

        8. Which entity owes the customer a refund?

        9. Which entity’s license, bond, and permissible investments cover the obligation?

        A bank-led model might provide a different licensing analysis if the bank

        - account segregation;

        - daily reconciliation;

        - shortfalls and reserve funding;

        - bank setoff rights;

        - liens and security interests;

        - continuity if the account is frozen;

        - treatment of pending transfers;

        - return and chargeback duties;

        - whether any deposit-insurance statement is accurate for the actual account

    available_excerpt: '“Operative authority” means the **current, effective law or
      regulator instruction that controls the proposed transaction in a specific state**.


      I did not retrieve and inspect an exact state provision that answers:


      - whether Harbor’s license can transfer in an asset sale;

      - whether Harbor or Mosaic must give notice or obtain approval;

      - whether Mosaic can operate while an application is pending;

      - whether Harbor can continue as the licensed provider after closing; or

      - when Harbor can surrender its license and bonds.


      The research found a **model act** and a general New York licensing page. A
      model act is suggested text. It applies only if a state enacted it, and the
      state can change the text. A general licensing page does not answer the transaction-specific
      questions above.


      The four Harbor-only states were also not identified. Therefore, I could not
      determine which enacted state laws or regulator procedures control.


      My earlier statement did **not** mean that no relevant law exists. It meant
      that the available research did not provide enough state-specific legal text
      to verify the conclusion. The recommendation was therefore cautious analysis,
      not a verified state-law determination.'

    path: 03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/conversations/CONV-20260909-5299cf.md
    available_excerpt: '# Draft dossier: Harbor Pay acquisition migration


      **Status:** Working draft. It uses the hypothetical assumption that a partner
      bank holds customer funds. It does not change the current transaction plan or
      record a decision.


      ## Executive summary


      The Harbor migration needs legal and compliance review before the planned November
      15 close.


      Mosaic should not:


      - assume that Harbor’s state licenses transfer in the asset purchase;

      - launch in the four Harbor-only states without confirmed authority;

      - rely on Harbor’s prior identity checks until compliance approves that approach;

      - migrate customers linked to unresolved alerts without review; or

      - depend on an untested day-one monitoring switch.


      A partner bank holding funds does not resolve these issues by itself. The key
      question is who provides the service, controls transfers, and owes the customer.


      **Working recommendation:** Separate the acquisition closing from the launch
      of regulated services. Block affected services where authority or compliance
      readiness is not confirmed.


      ## Reported facts


      - Mosaic is buying Harbor’s assets.

      - Closing is targeted for November 15.

      - About 240,000 consumers and 12,000 business accounts will move on day one.

      - Users will receive one account, one balance, and access to Mosaic’s products.

      - Mosaic plans no customer re-verification.

      - Pass/fail identity-check results exist, but some pre-2023 supporting documents
      are missing.

      - Harbor’s transaction-monitoring contract ends at close.

      - About 40 alerts and several suspicious activity reports remain unread.

      - Mosaic plans to use or assume Harbor’s licenses in four additional states.

      - Marketing proposes a $50 first-transfer bonus.


      ## Hypothetical assumption


      A partner bank holds customer funds.


      This does not establish that the bank is the legal service provider. That depends
      on the contracts and actual operations, including:


      - who contracts with users;

      - who accepts transfer instructions;

      - who controls settlement;

      - who records balances;

      - who owes refunds;

      - who handles complaints and losses; and

      - who performs customer checks, monitoring, alert review, and reporting.


      ## Main issues


      ### State licensing


      Harbor’s licenses should not be treated as assets that automatically move to
      Mosaic.


      The four Harbor-only states present the clearest launch risk. Mosaic should
      require its own authority, an approved transition, or another confirmed legal
      basis before offering regulated services there.


      The state review should cover asset-sale notices, buyer applications, changes
      in business plans, customer-obligation transfers, bonds, interim operations,
      and license surrender.


      **Support limit:** The four states have not been identified. No current state-specific
      rule or regulator instruction has been verified for this transaction.


      ### Partner-bank structure


      Bank custody can affect the analysis, but it is not automatically a licensing
      exemption.


      A genuine bank-led program could produce a different result if the bank is the
      actual provider and Mosaic performs only permitted support services. The contracts,
      customer terms, account control, and operations must all support that structure.


      ### Customer identification records


      Compliance has not confirmed whether Mosaic’s program permits reliance on Harbor’s
      prior checks.


      Before migration, compliance should determine:


      - whether reliance is permitted;

      - which records Mosaic must hold;

      - which customers need new checks;

      - whether missing records can be repaired; and

      - whether affected accounts need temporary restrictions.


      ### Alerts and suspicious activity reports


      The unread alerts and report history are a pre-close diligence priority.


      The review could identify unresolved suspicious activity, reporting gaps, restricted
      customers, or broader weaknesses in Harbor’s compliance program. Findings could
      affect customer migration, closing conditions, deal protections, or price.


      ### Monitoring transition


      Repointing Harbor’s transaction feed on day one does not prove that monitoring
      will work.


      Mosaic should test:


      - data completeness;

      - customer-history transfer;

      - monitoring-rule coverage;

      - alert generation;

      - case routing;

      - sanctions controls;

      - escalation procedures; and

      - the fallback process if the connection fails.


      ### Customer terms and communications


      The unified Mosaic account may change the service provider, contract, product
      set, fees, privacy terms, and complaint contact.


      Legal should review contract assignment, required notices, consent, balance
      presentation, privacy disclosures, and statements about the partner bank.


      ### Welcome bonus


      The $50 campaign needs review when its final terms are available.


      Marketing must define eligibility, qualifying transfers, payment timing, exclusions,
      expiration, and disclosures. The offer should not launch until the advertised
      benefit matches the actual product rules.


      ## Recommended pre-close conditions


      1. Identify the four Harbor-only states.

      2. Complete a state-by-state authority and filing matrix.

      3. Confirm the legal service provider after closing.

      4. Document the full funds and customer-obligation flow.

      5. Review the partner-bank agreements and required consents.

      6. Review the open alerts and suspicious activity report history.

      7. Decide how to handle customers with missing identity records.

      8. Test the monitoring transition and fallback.

      9. confirm customer contract and notice requirements.

      10. Add state and customer restrictions where needed.


      ## Fallback paths


      **State launch hold:** Close the acquisition, but block regulated services where
      Mosaic lacks confirmed authority.


      **Harbor transition:** Keep Harbor as the actual licensed provider temporarily,
      where the contracts, regulators, bank, and operations permit it.


      **Bank-led program:** Make the partner bank the actual provider. This would
      require material changes to contracts, controls, customer disclosures, and operations.


      ## Working conclusion


      The acquisition can potentially close before every customer is migrated. Mosaic
      should treat legal authority and compliance readiness as launch conditions.


      The safest working rule is:


      > **No confirmed authority and no tested compliance coverage means no day-one
      regulated service for the affected customer.**


      The next step is to identify the four Harbor-only states and prepare one combined
      licensing, funds-flow, and compliance-readiness matrix.'
    record_revision: 56bdbe93233356ee93ddea861ffcc416e077c58ed1b1e5a3f0d4df3fd721356b
    source_version: 56bdbe93233356ee93ddea861ffcc416e077c58ed1b1e5a3f0d4df3fd721356b
    support_state: supplied
    captured_record:
      message_id: MSG-20260911-e30dde

        The unified Mosaic account may change the service provider, contract, product
        set, fees, privacy terms, and complaint contact.


        Legal should review contract assignment, required notices, consent, balance
        presentation, privacy disclosures, and statements about the partner bank.


        ### Welcome bonus


        The $50 campaign needs review when its final terms are available.


        Marketing must define eligibility, qualifying transfers, payment timing, exclusions,
        expiration, and disclosures. The offer should not launch until the advertised
        benefit matches the actual product rules.


        ## Recommended pre-close conditions


        1. Identify the four Harbor-only states.

        2. Complete a state-by-state authority and filing matrix.

        3. Confirm the legal service provider after closing.

        4. Document the full funds and customer-obligation flow.

        5. Review the partner-bank agreements and required consents.

        6. Review the open alerts and suspicious activity report history.

        7. Decide how to handle customers with missing identity records.

        8. Test the monitoring transition and fallback.

        9. confirm customer contract and notice requirements.

        10. Add state and customer restrictions where needed.


        ## Fallback paths


        **State launch hold:** Close the acquisition, but block regulated services
        where Mosaic lacks confirmed authority.


        **Harbor transition:** Keep Harbor as the actual licensed provider temporarily,
        where the contracts, regulators, bank, and operations permit it.


        **Bank-led program:** Make the partner bank the actual provider. This would
        require material changes to contracts, controls, customer disclosures, and
        operations.


        ## Working conclusion


        The acquisition can potentially close before every customer is migrated. Mosaic
        should treat legal authority and compliance readiness as launch conditions.


        The safest working rule is:


        > **No confirmed authority and no tested compliance coverage means no day-one
        regulated service for the affected customer.**


        The next step is to identify the four Harbor-only states and prepare one combined
        licensing, funds-flow, and compliance-readiness matrix.'

    path: 03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/conversations/CONV-20260909-5299cf.md
    available_excerpt: '# Matter dossier — Harbor 2


      ## Current position


      Mosaic Relay is acquiring Harbor Pay in an asset purchase targeted to close
      Nov 15 (~10 weeks out). The migration plan moves 240,000 consumer and 12,000
      business accounts into Mosaic''s app on day 1, relying on Harbor''s prior KYC
      (with missing underlying documents for pre-2023 users), assuming Harbor''s 38
      state MTLs to create a combined 44-state footprint, and launching a $50 welcome
      bonus campaign. Harbor has ~40 open alerts and unreviewed SARs, and its transaction-monitoring
      SaaS contract terminates at close.


      **The migration needs legal and compliance review before close.** The leading
      concern is that an asset purchase generally requires Mosaic Relay to confirm
      its own authority to operate in each state; the plan to use or assume Harbor''s
      licenses is not established as legally effective. The most important unresolved
      decision is whether to hold the regulated launch in the four Harbor-only states
      until Mosaic has documented authority, and whether the ~40 open alerts and SARs
      reveal BSA/AML exposure that must be addressed before close.


      ## Decision question


      Does any part of the Harbor Pay acquisition migration need legal review before
      the Nov 15 close?


      ## Issues


      <!-- issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-94e42e2b38090045 -->

      ### Money transmission licensing: whether Mosaic Relay can operate under the
      combined 44-state footprint and whether Harbor''s MTLs transfer/survive the
      acquisition


      **Current answer**


      Do not rely on transferability without an applicable state rule, approval, or
      written regulator position. The answer must be determined state by state and
      must cover more than technical change-of-control rules. This path is possible
      only if Harbor remains the real provider and the relevant states permit the
      arrangement. The unified Mosaic account design may conflict with that position.
      The result depends on the contracts and actual funds flow. Custody alone should
      not be treated as a licensing exemption or transfer mechanism.


      **Rule and support**


      State money transmitter licenses are entity-specific. An asset purchase does
      not automatically transfer Harbor''s licenses to Mosaic. The CSBS Money Transmission
      Modernization Act is a model act, not proof of the law adopted in any specific
      state, and no exact operative provision about asset sales, license transfer,
      interim operation, or custody was available for review. [source:SRC-37ee676da5b354ba8538]
      (retrieved; passage not reviewed). The New York DFS licensing page confirms
      that money transmitter licensing is a state regulatory process but does not
      address transferability in an acquisition. [source:SRC-71b20ecf65825d6522c4]
      (retrieved; passage not reviewed). The OCC interpretive letter on National Bank
      Act preemption is not applicable here — Mosaic Relay is not a national bank.
      [source:SRC-e6c1e4e85e004ccbe413] (retrieved; passage not reviewed).


      **Application**


      The acquisition is confirmed as an asset purchase (FACT-20260909-ed9f06). Mosaic
      plans to use or assume Harbor''s licenses for the four new markets (FACT-20260909-f0fb01,
      FACT-20260909-771ee1). Because an asset purchase does not change Harbor''s ownership,
      a technical "change of control" rule may not even apply — but that does not
      resolve the issue. Harbor may still need a notice or surrender approval, and
      Mosaic may separately need authority before it starts regulated activity. The
      planned unified Mosaic account (one account, one balance, full product access)
      suggests Mosaic, not Harbor, will be the actual provider post-close, which makes
      it harder to show Harbor remains the true licensed provider under any interim
      structure. Partner-bank custody must be reviewed, but a bank account alone does
      not establish Mosaic''s authority.


      **Next step**


      Identify the four Harbor-only states and complete one matrix covering license
      authority, transaction filings, interim operation, bank custody, outstanding
      obligations, and license surrender. Until Mosaic has documented authority in
      a state, hold the regulated launch there.


      <!-- issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-ca95e529feeb1045 -->

      ### BSA/AML and KYC: whether relying on Harbor''s prior KYC (with missing underlying
      documents for pre-2023 users) satisfies Mosaic Relay''s independent CIP/AML
      obligations


      **Current answer**


      Not yet established. Mosaic Relay has not confirmed whether its AML/CIP program
      permits reliance on Harbor''s prior KYC, and underlying records are missing
      for some pre-2023 users. These gaps make a day-one migration without re-verification
      or account restrictions difficult to support.


      **Rule and support**


      No research update has been saved for this issue. The analysis relies on the
      reported facts and the general principle that a money services business must
      maintain its own BSA/AML program, including customer identification and recordkeeping
      obligations. No external authority has been retrieved to support a specific
      reliance standard.


      **Application**


      The plan is no re-verification; Harbor users were already KYC''d by Harbor (FACT-20260909-9a0c61,
      FACT-20260909-f2c49f). Harbor''s KYC ran through a vendor that shut down in
      2024; Mosaic has pass/fail results for everyone but not the underlying documents
      for users onboarded before 2023 (FACT-20260909-aea8ec, FACT-20260909-13d4c1).
      Mosaic Relay''s compliance has not confirmed whether its AML/CIP program permits
      reliance on Harbor''s prior KYC (FACT-20260909-b5e71e, FACT-20260909-620c62).
      Whether the missing underlying documents create a compliance gap depends on
      how Mosaic plans to satisfy its own CIP/recordkeeping obligations for these
      users.


      **Next step**


      Compliance must confirm whether Mosaic Relay''s AML/CIP program permits reliance
      on Harbor''s prior KYC without underlying documents, and if not, what remediation
      (re-verification, account restrictions, or document recovery) is required before
      migrated users receive full access.


      <!-- issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-69af3f282d21ef38 -->

      ### SAR and alert handling: obligations to review, file, and manage Harbor''s
      ~40 open alerts and SARs, and successor liability


      **Current answer**


      The open alerts and SAR history have not been reviewed and remain in diligence.
      This is the highest-risk item; unread open alerts and SARs can create BSA/AML
      exposure that must be addressed before close and could affect the deal itself.


      **Rule and support**


      No research update has been saved for this issue. No external authority has
      been retrieved on successor liability for SARs and alerts in an asset acquisition.


      **Application**


      Diligence flagged ~40 open alerts and "a handful" of SARs from the last year
      at Harbor; nobody has read them yet (FACT-20260909-adf2cb, FACT-20260909-9f7bde,
      FACT-20260909-849b00, FACT-20260909-070aec). The nature, age, and whether any
      are unresolved or reportable remain unknown. In an asset purchase, the question
      of which entity inherits the obligation to review, file, and manage these items
      depends on the deal structure and the allocation of regulatory obligations —
      which has not been determined.


      **Next step**


      Review the ~40 open alerts and the handful of SARs before close. Determine whether
      any are unresolved or reportable, and confirm which entity bears the obligation
      to complete, file, or manage them post-close.


      <!-- issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-4825f0af7ec75542 -->

      ### Monitoring vendor transition: continuity of transaction monitoring when
      the SaaS contract terminates at close


      **Current answer**


      Transaction monitoring continuity is material because Harbor''s vendor ends
      at close. The repointed feed should be tested and validated before migrated
      users receive full access.


      **Rule and support**


      No research update has been saved for this issue. No external authority has
      been retrieved on monitoring continuity obligations in an acquisition.


      **Application**


      Harbor''s transaction monitoring is a third-party SaaS whose contract terminates
      at close; the plan is to repoint Harbor''s transaction feed at Mosaic''s engine
      on day 1 (FACT-20260909-073838, FACT-20260909-78d5cd). The plan assumes the
      repoint can be completed and validated before close without a monitoring gap
      (ASM-20260909-1a519f, open). A day-one repoint without prior testing and validation
      creates a risk of a monitoring gap for migrated users.


      **Next step**


      Test and validate the repointed feed before migrated users receive full access.
      Confirm there is no monitoring gap between the SaaS termination at close and
      the day-1 repoint.


      <!-- issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-72810d0a43d3ce66 -->

      ### Marketing compliance: the $50 welcome bonus offer and any state money transmission
      or consumer protection implications


      **Current answer**


      The $50 bonus is lower priority but still needs review once its eligibility,
      funding, terms, and disclosures are known.


      **Rule and support**


      No research update has been saved for this issue. No external authority has
      been retrieved on promotional bonus offers in the money transmission context.


      **Application**


      Marketing wants a "Welcome" campaign with a $50 bonus on first transfer, launching
      close+1 (FACT-20260909-19aebc). The structure of the bonus is unknown — whether
      it is a deposit, incentive, or referral, and whether it triggers disclosure
      or state requirements (Q-BONUS, left open). The bonus structure determines whether
      it triggers consumer disclosure, funds-flow, or state promotional requirements.


      **Next step**


      Obtain the bonus structure (eligibility, funding, terms, disclosures) and review
      for state money transmission and consumer protection implications before launch.


      ## Next actions


      1. **Identify the four Harbor-only states and complete one state-by-state licensing
      matrix** covering license authority, transaction filings, interim operation,
      bank custody, outstanding obligations, and license surrender. — Proposed owner:
      Licensing team / state regulatory counsel. Proposed timing: before closing conditions
      are final. Evidence needed: confirmed license list, applicable state rules,
      filing deadlines, regulator responses. This is the gating regulatory question
      for the four new markets. (Proposed; no owner or due date recorded.)

      2. **Review the ~40 open alerts and the handful of SARs from the last year.**
      — Proposed owner: compliance/diligence. Evidence needed: nature, age, and reportability
      of each alert and SAR; determination of which entity bears post-close obligations.
      This is the highest-risk BSA/AML item. (Proposed; no owner or due date recorded.)

      3. **Compliance confirms whether Mosaic Relay''s AML/CIP program permits reliance
      on Harbor''s prior KYC** without underlying documents, and what remediation
      is required for pre-2023 users. — Proposed owner: compliance. Evidence needed:
      AML/CIP program terms, remediation plan. (Proposed; no owner or due date recorded.)

      4. **Test and validate the transaction monitoring repoint** before migrated
      users receive full access. — Proposed owner: product/engineering with compliance.
      Evidence needed: test results showing no monitoring gap. (Proposed; no owner
      or due date recorded.)

      5. **Obtain the $50 welcome bonus structure** (eligibility, funding, terms,
      disclosures) and review for state money transmission and consumer protection
      implications. — Proposed owner: marketing with legal. Evidence needed: campaign
      terms. (Proposed; no owner or due date recorded.)


      ## Work product and supporting records


      - **Prior dossier revision:** `03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/dossier-revisions/DOS-c993ab2326efd727cc3d.md`

      - **Latest research:** `03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/RES-20260911-0a5f07.md`
      — 4 sources retrieved; 0 with passages read. No verified operative authority
      retrieved for the licensing issue.

      - **Source library:** `03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/source-library/index.md`

      - **Facts record:** `03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/facts.md`

      - **Conversation transcript:** `03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/conversations/CONV-20260909-5299cf.md`

      - **Work items:** `WI-20260909-dc797b` (Orient to the request — done)

      - **No work product yet.**


      ## Assumptions used


      The following open assumptions are material to the analysis. They are not confirmed
      facts:


      - **ASM-20260909-29b823 (open):** Mosaic Relay''s existing money transmission
      licenses and Harbor''s MTLs are transferable or can be maintained through the
      acquisition without new state approval. The licensing issue depends on this;
      the current analysis does not assume it is true.

      - **ASM-20260909-3c5756 (open):** The KYC pass/fail results are sufficient to
      satisfy Mosaic Relay''s own BSA/AML program obligations without the underlying
      documents. The KYC issue depends on this; compliance must confirm.

      - **ASM-20260909-1a519f (open):** The transaction monitoring repoint can be
      completed and validated before close without a monitoring gap. The monitoring
      issue depends on this.

      - **ASM-20260909-ca7dfd (open):** Harbor Pay is a licensed money transmitter
      subject to state MTL regimes and federal BSA/AML obligations (not yet confirmed).

      - **ASM-20260909-d40239 (open):** Mosaic Relay is itself a licensed money transmitter
      in 41 states (per the request).

      - **ASM-20260909-57e86b (open):** The acquisition is structured as an asset
      or stock purchase, which affects whether Harbor''s licenses and SAR/alert obligations
      transfer. This is now partially resolved — the acquisition is confirmed as an
      asset purchase (FACT-20260909-ed9f06) — but the effect on license portability
      and regulatory obligations remains unresolved.


      ## Current solution direction


      Current direction: Mosaic-operated migration (SCN-490c71c86d9c658974321c1e).
      Previous approach: SCN-406906c91ab0aba4313b54a6.


      Dossier preview generated; no matter records changed.'
    record_revision: 54cf9b63ac3d396e596068c66424d9225bb581ac0c517a4d8d265d10fa9961a5

    available_excerpt: '# Matter dossier


      ## Matter summary


      Mosaic Relay is acquiring Harbor Pay in an asset purchase targeted to close
      Nov 15 (~10 weeks out). The plan involves migrating 240,000 consumer and 12,000
      business accounts into Mosaic’s app on day 1, relying on Harbor’s prior KYC
      (for which underlying documents are missing for pre-2023 users), assuming Harbor’s
      38 state MTLs to create a combined 44-state footprint, and launching a $50 welcome
      bonus campaign. Harbor has ~40 open alerts and unreviewed SARs, and its transaction-monitoring
      SaaS contract terminates at close.


      ## Decision question


      Does any part of the Harbor Pay acquisition migration need legal review before
      the Nov 15 close?


      ## Material facts


      - Mosaic Relay is acquiring Harbor Pay; close targeted Nov 15 (~10 weeks out).

      - Migration moves 240k consumer users and 12k business accounts into Mosaic
      Relay''s app on day 1 post-close, with one unified account and balance and full
      access to Mosaic''s product set.

      - Plan is no re-verification; Harbor users were already KYC''d by Harbor.

      - Harbor holds money transmitter licenses (MTLs) in 38 states; Mosaic Relay
      holds 41; combined footprint is 44 states, opening four new markets.

      - Harbor''s KYC ran through a vendor that shut down in 2024; Mosaic has pass/fail
      results for everyone but not the underlying documents for users onboarded before
      2023.

      - Harbor''s transaction monitoring is a third-party SaaS whose contract terminates
      at close; plan is to repoint Harbor''s transaction feed at Mosaic''s engine
      on day 1.

      - Marketing wants a ''Welcome'' campaign with a $50 bonus on first transfer,
      launching close+1.

      - Diligence flagged ~40 open alerts and ''a handful'' of SARs from the last
      year at Harbor; nobody has read them yet.


      ## Assumptions


      This answer did not identify any assumptions it relies on. Reported facts remain
      in Material facts.


      <details>

      <summary>Earlier assumptions awaiting reconciliation</summary>


      These saved assumptions have not been reconciled with this answer. They do not
      override later reported facts.


      - Generated assumption (2026-09-09): Mosaic Relay''s existing money transmission
      licenses and Harbor''s MTLs are transferable or can be maintained through the
      acquisition without new state approval

      - Generated assumption (2026-09-09): The KYC pass/fail results are sufficient
      to satisfy Mosaic Relay''s own BSA/AML program obligations without the underlying
      documents

      - Generated assumption (2026-09-09): The transaction monitoring repoint can
      be completed and validated before close without a monitoring gap

      - Generated assumption (2026-09-09): Harbor Pay is a licensed money transmitter
      subject to state MTL regimes and federal BSA/AML obligations (not yet confirmed).

      - Generated assumption (2026-09-09): Mosaic Relay is itself a licensed money
      transmitter in 41 states (per the request).

      - Generated assumption (2026-09-09): The acquisition is structured as an asset
      or stock purchase, which affects whether Harbor''s licenses and SAR/alert obligations
      transfer (structure not yet confirmed).


      </details>


      ## Issues and workstreams


      - Money transmission licensing and state regulatory approval for the four new
      markets and the combined footprint

      - BSA/AML program continuity: KYC records gap (missing underlying documents
      pre-2023), monitoring repoint, and unread open alerts and SARs

      - Consumer protection and funds-flow disclosures for migrated accounts

      - Marketing compliance for the $50 welcome bonus campaign

      - Data migration and privacy/data protection obligations for transferring customer
      personal data


      ## Open questions


      - Whether the four new markets require new state money transmission licenses
      or regulatory approval before onboarding those customers

      - Whether the ~40 open alerts and SARs indicate unresolved suspicious activity
      that must be addressed before or at close

      - Whether the missing pre-2023 KYC underlying documents create a compliance
      gap under Mosaic Relay''s BSA/AML program

      - Whether the $50 welcome bonus is structured as a deposit, incentive, or referral
      and whether it triggers disclosure or state requirements

      - Whether the acquisition structure (asset vs. stock) affects license portability
      and regulatory approval


      ## Research and source support


      Latest review: `03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/Reference unavailable: RES-20260911-0a5f07.md`


      Retrieval alone does not establish claim support.


      <details>

      ## Options or working recommendation


      ### Latest research-based proposal — not yet accepted


      ### Saved overall view


      The Harbor Pay migration needs legal and compliance review before the planned
      close. The leading concern is that an asset purchase generally requires Mosaic
      Relay to confirm its own authority to operate in each state; the current plan
      to use or assume Harbor''s licenses is not established as legally effective.


      The second concern is BSA/AML readiness. Mosaic Relay has not confirmed whether
      its AML/CIP program permits reliance on Harbor''s prior KYC, and underlying
      records are missing for some pre-2023 users. Harbor''s open alerts and SAR history
      also remain unread. These gaps make a day-one migration without re-verification
      or account restrictions difficult to support.


      Transaction monitoring continuity is also material because Harbor''s vendor
      ends at close. The repointed feed should be tested and validated before migrated
      users receive full access. The $50 bonus is lower priority but still needs review
      once its eligibility, funding, terms, and disclosures are known.


      ### Workstream positions


      Research progress is separate from the lawyer''s conclusion.

      or written regulator position.  The answer must be determined state by state
      and must cover more than technical change-of-control rules.  This path is possible
      only if Harbor remains the real provider and the relevant states permit the
      arrangement. The unified Mosaic account design may conflict with that position.  The
      result depends on the contracts and actual funds flow. Custody alone should
      not be treated as a licensing exemption or transfer mechanism. Next: Identify

      transaction filings, interim operation, bank custody, outstanding obligations,
      and license surrender. [Full research](03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/RES-20260911-0a5f07.md)
      | Research added. 4 sources retrieved; 0 with passages read. |

      | BSA/AML and KYC: whether relying on Harbor''s prior KYC (with missing underlying
      documents for pre-2023 users) satisfies Mosaic Relay''s independent CIP/AML
      obligations | No separate issue position saved. The saved overall view remains
      available. | No research update saved. |

      | SAR and alert handling: obligations to review, file, and manage Harbor''s
      ~40 open alerts and SARs, and successor liability | No separate issue position

      |

      | Monitoring vendor transition: continuity of transaction monitoring when the
      SaaS contract terminates at close | No separate issue position saved. The saved

      ### Latest research update


      Research updated: Money transmission licensing: whether Mosaic Relay can operate

      <details>

      <summary>Earlier saved position — 2026-09-09T20:57:53+00:00</summary>


      **Working position**


      The Harbor Pay migration needs legal and compliance review before the planned
      close. The leading concern is that an asset purchase generally requires Mosaic
      Relay to confirm its own authority to operate in each state; the current plan
      to use or assume Harbor''s licenses is not established as legally effective.


      The second concern is BSA/AML readiness. Mosaic Relay has not confirmed whether
      its AML/CIP program permits reliance on Harbor''s prior KYC, and underlying
      records are missing for some pre-2023 users. Harbor''s open alerts and SAR history
      also remain unread. These gaps make a day-one migration without re-verification
      or account restrictions difficult to support.


      Transaction monitoring continuity is also material because Harbor''s vendor
      ends at close. The repointed feed should be tested and validated before migrated
      users receive full access. The $50 bonus is lower priority but still needs review
      once its eligibility, funding, terms, and disclosures are known.


      **Plausible alternatives**


      A narrower launch could exclude the four new markets, restrict users whose KYC
      evidence is incomplete, or delay full product access until alert review and
      monitoring validation finish. A full day-one launch remains possible only if
      state licensing, AML/CIP reliance, alert handling, and monitoring continuity
      are confirmed.


      **Material facts and assumptions**


      Reported facts include the asset-purchase structure, the current plan to rely
      on Harbor''s licenses, the lack of a compliance answer on KYC reliance, and
      the unread alerts and SAR history. The analysis assumes both entities conduct
      regulated money-transmission activity and that Mosaic Relay will become the
      operating provider immediately after close. It does not assume that Harbor''s
      licenses transfer or that Harbor''s KYC is sufficient.


      **What could change this view**


      The view could change if state counsel confirms an effective licensing path
      for each affected state, compliance confirms permissible reliance with adequate
      records or remediation, diligence clears the alerts and SAR history, and testing
      shows no monitoring gap. The bonus structure remains unknown.


      **Support limit**


      This is generated analysis based on reported matter facts. No external authority
      was retrieved.
