      ## Saved issue answers


      <!-- issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-94e42e2b38090045 -->

      ### Money transmission licensing: whether Mosaic Relay can operate under the
      combined 44-state footprint and whether Harbor''s MTLs transfer/survive the
      acquisition


      Do not rely on transferability without an applicable state rule, approval, or
      written regulator position.


      The answer must be determined state by state and must cover more than technical
      change-of-control rules.


      This path is possible only if Harbor remains the real provider and the relevant
      states permit the arrangement. The unified Mosaic account design may conflict
      with that position.


      The result depends on the contracts and actual funds flow. Custody alone should
      not be treated as a licensing exemption or transfer mechanism.


      Research saved. 4 sources retrieved; 0 with passages read.


      Next step: Identify the four Harbor-only states and complete one matrix covering
      license authority, transaction filings, interim operation, bank custody, outstanding
      obligations, and license surrender.


      <details>

      <summary>Detailed analysis</summary>


      **Practical answer**


      **Do not treat Harbor’s money transmitter licenses as assets that move to Mosaic
      in the asset purchase.** Use this closing rule:


      > **No confirmed authority means no regulated launch by Mosaic in that state.**


      The asset purchase can still close. However, Mosaic should not activate regulated
      services in the four Harbor-only states unless one of these conditions is met:


      1. Mosaic obtains its own license.

      2. The state approves a transfer or other license change.

      3. The regulator confirms a lawful interim structure.

      4. Harbor remains the real licensed provider under a valid transition plan.

      5. Mosaic blocks regulated functions in that state.


      The planned unified Mosaic account is important. It suggests that Mosaic, not
      Harbor, will become the service provider. If that is correct, keeping Harbor’s
      legal entity and licenses alive might not give Mosaic authority to operate.


      **No verified operative authority retrieved.** The collection found the CSBS
      Model Money Transmission Modernization Act and a New York regulator licensing
      page. However, no exact provision about an asset sale, license transfer, interim
      operation, or custody was available for review. Also, a model act is not proof
      of the law adopted in a specific state. The state-specific conclusions below
      remain conditional.


      **Work needed before launch**


      | Work | Why | Proposed owner | Needed by | Evidence to proceed | Fallback |

      |---|---|---|---|---|---|

      | Identify the four Harbor-only states | These states have the clearest authority
      gap | Licensing team | Now | Confirmed license list and covered activities |
      Block regulated functions in those states |

      | Build a matrix for every affected state | Transfer, notice, approval, and
      surrender rules can differ | State regulatory counsel | Before the closing conditions
      are final | Applicable rule, filing deadline, regulator response, and license
      status | Delay migration in the affected state |

      | Confirm the post-close service provider | The licensed entity must match the
      actual operating model | Product and deal counsel | Before customer notices
      and account design are final | Contracts, user interface, funds-flow diagram,
      and operating roles | Keep Harbor as the actual provider where permitted |

      | Review notices and approvals | An asset sale can create separate seller and
      buyer requirements | Licensing team | Before each state deadline | Filing receipts
      and required approvals or non-objections | Make approval a state launch condition
      |

      | Confirm interim operating authority | A pending application should not be
      assumed to permit operation | State regulatory counsel | Before close | Applicable
      rule or written regulator confirmation | Keep Harbor customers segregated or
      restrict transactions |

      | Review partner-bank arrangements | Bank custody does not, by itself, establish
      Mosaic’s licensing position | Payments counsel and bank relationship owner |
      Before balances move | Bank agreements, account titles, control rights, reconciliation
      design, and bank consent | Keep funds and obligations in the existing structure
      |

      | Prepare license run-off and surrender plans | Harbor may retain duties for
      balances, claims, records, and reports | Harbor compliance and deal counsel
      | Before surrender or operational shutdown | Regulator-approved plan and continued
      bonds and financial coverage | Keep Harbor licensed and operational longer |


      **State notice and approval**


      Do not search only for a rule called “license transfer.” Review each state for:


      - sale of substantially all assets;

      - change of control;

      - change in business plan;

      - change in licensed activities;

      - assignment of outstanding payment obligations;

      - change in responsible persons, locations, or agents;

      - license surrender or termination;

      - customer agreement assignment; and

      - movement of stored balances or customer funds.


      An asset purchase might fall outside a technical “change of control” rule because
      Harbor’s ownership does not change. That does not resolve the issue. Harbor
      might still need a notice or surrender approval. Mosaic might separately need
      authority before it starts the regulated activity.


      The state matrix should distinguish:


      - notice only;

      - approval before closing;

      - approval before customer migration;

      - new license required;

      - operation permitted while an application is pending;

      - regulator-approved transition available; and

      - no supported interim path.


      **Interim operation**


      Harbor could remain the licensed provider for a transition period. However,
      the structure must be real, not only contractual language.


      A supportable structure would normally need to show that:


      - Harbor remains responsible to customers.

      - Customer terms name Harbor as the provider.

      - Harbor controls the regulated activity as required.

      - Harbor’s licenses, bonds, and financial coverage remain active.

      - Customer funds and outstanding obligations remain covered.

      - Harbor can complete reports, refunds, complaints, and examinations.

      - Required records remain available.

      - Mosaic’s operational role is permitted.

      - The partner bank accepts the structure.

      - Each relevant regulator permits or accepts the structure.


      The present plan creates tension with this approach. A single Mosaic account,
      one combined balance, and full access to Mosaic products make it harder to show
      that Harbor remains the true provider.


      **Partner-bank custody**


      A partner bank account is not automatically a substitute for a money transmitter
      license. The key questions concern the complete activity and obligation flow.


      For each customer balance and transfer, document:


      1. Who receives the customer’s instruction?

      2. Who accepts the obligation to transmit or return the funds?

      3. In whose name is the bank account held?

      4. Is it a custodial or “for benefit of” account?

      5. Who can direct withdrawals?

      6. Who owns the ledger and resolves errors?

      7. Who bears loss if a transfer fails?

      8. Which entity owes the customer a refund?

      9. Which entity’s license, bond, and permissible investments cover the obligation?

      10. Does the bank agreement permit assignment, migration, or a change in the
      program manager?

      11. Is bank consent required before closing or migration?

      12. What happens to funds if Harbor, Mosaic, or the bank relationship ends?


      A bank-led model might provide a different licensing analysis if the bank is
      the actual provider and Mosaic performs only permitted services. A bank account
      that merely holds funds for Mosaic or Harbor is different. The legal documents,
      customer terms, control rights, and actual operations must all support the claimed
      model.


      Also review:


      - account segregation;

      - daily reconciliation;

      - shortfalls and reserve funding;

      - bank setoff rights;

      - liens and security interests;

      - continuity if the account is frozen;

      - treatment of pending transfers;

      - return and chargeback duties;

      - insolvency treatment;

      - customer disclosures; and

      - whether any deposit-insurance statement is accurate for the actual account
      structure.


      **Recommended closing position**


      Keep the acquisition closing separate from the regulated-service launch.


      For the four Harbor-only states, use a state launch hold unless Mosaic has documented
      authority. For overlapping states, confirm that Mosaic’s existing licenses cover
      the complete post-close product set and funds flow.


      Do not surrender Harbor’s licenses or release its bonds until the applicable
      regulators permit it and all outstanding obligations have been assigned, completed,
      or placed into an approved run-off structure.


      This investigation does not change the current recommendation. It adds one material
      point: **partner-bank custody is part of the licensing analysis, but custody
      alone does not cure an authority gap.**


      </details>

        gap under Mosaic Relay''s BSA/AML program

        - Whether the $50 welcome bonus is structured as a deposit, incentive, or
        referral and whether it triggers disclosure or state requirements

        - Whether the acquisition structure (asset vs. stock) affects license portability
        and regulatory approval

        <details>

        <summary>Earlier saved position — 2026-09-09T20:57:53+00:00</summary>


        **Working position**


        The Harbor Pay migration needs legal and compliance review before the planned
        close. The leading concern is that an asset purchase generally requires Mosaic
        Relay to confirm its own authority to operate in each state; the current plan
        to use or assume Harbor''s licenses is not established as legally effective.


        The second concern is BSA/AML readiness. Mosaic Relay has not confirmed whether
        its AML/CIP program permits reliance on Harbor''s prior KYC, and underlying
        records are missing for some pre-2023 users. Harbor''s open alerts and SAR
        history also remain unread. These gaps make a day-one migration without re-verification
        or account restrictions difficult to support.


        Transaction monitoring continuity is also material because Harbor''s vendor
        ends at close. The repointed feed should be tested and validated before migrated
        users receive full access. The $50 bonus is lower priority but still needs
        review once its eligibility, funding, terms, and disclosures are known.


        **Plausible alternatives**


        A narrower launch could exclude the four new markets, restrict users whose
        KYC evidence is incomplete, or delay full product access until alert review
        and monitoring validation finish. A full day-one launch remains possible only
        if state licensing, AML/CIP reliance, alert handling, and monitoring continuity
        are confirmed.


        **Material facts and assumptions**


        Reported facts include the asset-purchase structure, the current plan to rely
        on Harbor''s licenses, the lack of a compliance answer on KYC reliance, and
        the unread alerts and SAR history. The analysis assumes both entities conduct
        regulated money-transmission activity and that Mosaic Relay will become the
        operating provider immediately after close. It does not assume that Harbor''s
        licenses transfer or that Harbor''s KYC is sufficient.


        **What could change this view**


        The view could change if state counsel confirms an effective licensing path
        for each affected state, compliance confirms permissible reliance with adequate
        records or remediation, diligence clears the alerts and SAR history, and testing
        shows no monitoring gap. The bonus structure remains unknown.


        **Support limit**


        This is generated analysis based on reported matter facts. No external authority
        was retrieved.


        </details>

        ## Saved issue answers


        <!-- issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-94e42e2b38090045 -->

        ### Money transmission licensing: whether Mosaic Relay can operate under the
        combined 44-state footprint and whether Harbor''s MTLs transfer/survive the
        acquisition


        Do not rely on transferability without an applicable state rule, approval,
        or written regulator position.


        The answer must be determined state by state and must cover more than technical
        change-of-control rules.


        This path is possible only if Harbor remains the real provider and the relevant
        states permit the arrangement. The unified Mosaic account design may conflict
        with that position.


        The result depends on the contracts and actual funds flow. Custody alone should
        not be treated as a licensing exemption or transfer mechanism.


        Research saved. 4 sources retrieved; 0 with passages read.


        Next step: Identify the four Harbor-only states and complete one matrix covering
        license authority, transaction filings, interim operation, bank custody, outstanding
        obligations, and license surrender.


        <details>

        <summary>Detailed analysis</summary>


        **Practical answer**


        **Do not treat Harbor’s money transmitter licenses as assets that move to
        Mosaic in the asset purchase.** Use this closing rule:


        > **No confirmed authority means no regulated launch by Mosaic in that state.**


        The asset purchase can still close. However, Mosaic should not activate regulated
        services in the four Harbor-only states unless one of these conditions is
        met:


        1. Mosaic obtains its own license.

        2. The state approves a transfer or other license change.

        3. The regulator confirms a lawful interim structure.

        4. Harbor remains the real licensed provider under a valid transition plan.

        5. Mosaic blocks regulated functions in that state.


        The planned unified Mosaic account is important. It suggests that Mosaic,
        not Harbor, will become the service provider. If that is correct, keeping
        Harbor’s legal entity and licenses alive might not give Mosaic authority to
        operate.


        **No verified operative authority retrieved.** The collection found the CSBS
        Model Money Transmission Modernization Act and a New York regulator licensing
        page. However, no exact provision about an asset sale, license transfer, interim
        operation, or custody was available for review. Also, a model act is not proof
        of the law adopted in a specific state. The state-specific conclusions below
        remain conditional.


        **Work needed before launch**


        | Work | Why | Proposed owner | Needed by | Evidence to proceed | Fallback
        |

        |---|---|---|---|---|---|

        | Identify the four Harbor-only states | These states have the clearest authority
        gap | Licensing team | Now | Confirmed license list and covered activities
        | Block regulated functions in those states |

        | Build a matrix for every affected state | Transfer, notice, approval, and
        surrender rules can differ | State regulatory counsel | Before the closing
        conditions are final | Applicable rule, filing deadline, regulator response,
        and license status | Delay migration in the affected state |

        | Confirm the post-close service provider | The licensed entity must match
        the actual operating model | Product and deal counsel | Before customer notices
        and account design are final | Contracts, user interface, funds-flow diagram,
        and operating roles | Keep Harbor as the actual provider where permitted |

        | Review notices and approvals | An asset sale can create separate seller
        and buyer requirements | Licensing team | Before each state deadline | Filing
        receipts and required approvals or non-objections | Make approval a state
        launch condition |

        | Confirm interim operating authority | A pending application should not be
        assumed to permit operation | State regulatory counsel | Before close | Applicable
        rule or written regulator confirmation | Keep Harbor customers segregated
        or restrict transactions |

        | Review partner-bank arrangements | Bank custody does not, by itself, establish
        Mosaic’s licensing position | Payments counsel and bank relationship owner
        | Before balances move | Bank agreements, account titles, control rights,
        reconciliation design, and bank consent | Keep funds and obligations in the
        existing structure |

        | Prepare license run-off and surrender plans | Harbor may retain duties for
        balances, claims, records, and reports | Harbor compliance and deal counsel
        | Before surrender or operational shutdown | Regulator-approved plan and continued
        bonds and financial coverage | Keep Harbor licensed and operational longer
        |


        **State notice and approval**


        Do not search only for a rule called “license transfer.” Review each state
        for:


        - sale of substantially all assets;

        - change of control;

        - change in business plan;

        - change in licensed activities;

        - assignment of outstanding payment obligations;

        - change in responsible persons, locations, or agents;

        - license surrender or termination;

        - customer agreement assignment; and

        - movement of stored balances or customer funds.


        An asset purchase might fall outside a technical “change of control” rule
        because Harbor’s ownership does not change. That does not resolve the issue.
        Harbor might still need a notice or surrender approval. Mosaic might separately
        need authority before it starts the regulated activity.


        The state matrix should distinguish:


        - notice only;

        - approval before closing;

        - approval before customer migration;

        - new license required;

        - operation permitted while an application is pending;

        - regulator-approved transition available; and

        - no supported interim path.


        **Interim operation**


        Harbor could remain the licensed provider for a transition period. However,
        the structure must be real, not only contractual language.


        A supportable structure would normally need to show that:


        - Harbor remains responsible to customers.

        - Customer terms name Harbor as the provider.

        - Harbor controls the regulated activity as required.

        - Harbor’s licenses, bonds, and financial coverage remain active.

        - Customer funds and outstanding obligations remain covered.

        - Harbor can complete reports, refunds, complaints, and examinations.

        - Required records remain available.

        - Mosaic’s operational role is permitted.

        - The partner bank accepts the structure.

        - Each relevant regulator permits or accepts the structure.


        The present plan creates tension with this approach. A single Mosaic account,
        one combined balance, and full access to Mosaic products make it harder to
        show that Harbor remains the true provider.


        **Partner-bank custody**


        A partner bank account is not automatically a substitute for a money transmitter
        license. The key questions concern the complete activity and obligation flow.


        For each customer balance and transfer, document:


        1. Who receives the customer’s instruction?

        2. Who accepts the obligation to transmit or return the funds?

        3. In whose name is the bank account held?

        4. Is it a custodial or “for benefit of” account?

        5. Who can direct withdrawals?

        6. Who owns the ledger and resolves errors?

        7. Who bears loss if a transfer fails?

        8. Which entity owes the customer a refund?

        9. Which entity’s license, bond, and permissible investments cover the obligation?

        10. Does the bank agreement permit assignment, migration, or a change in the
        program manager?

        11. Is bank consent required before closing or migration?

        12. What happens to funds if Harbor, Mosaic, or the bank relationship ends?


        A bank-led model might provide a different licensing analysis if the bank
        is the actual provider and Mosaic performs only permitted services. A bank
        account that merely holds funds for Mosaic or Harbor is different. The legal
        documents, customer terms, control rights, and actual operations must all
        support the claimed model.


        Also review:


        - account segregation;

        - daily reconciliation;

        - shortfalls and reserve funding;

        - bank setoff rights;

        - liens and security interests;

        - continuity if the account is frozen;

        - treatment of pending transfers;

        - return and chargeback duties;

        - insolvency treatment;

        - customer disclosures; and

        - whether any deposit-insurance statement is accurate for the actual account
        structure.


        **Recommended closing position**


        Keep the acquisition closing separate from the regulated-service launch.


        For the four Harbor-only states, use a state launch hold unless Mosaic has
        documented authority. For overlapping states, confirm that Mosaic’s existing
        licenses cover the complete post-close product set and funds flow.


        Do not surrender Harbor’s licenses or release its bonds until the applicable
        regulators permit it and all outstanding obligations have been assigned, completed,
        or placed into an approved run-off structure.


        This investigation does not change the current recommendation. It adds one
        material point: **partner-bank custody is part of the licensing analysis,
        but custody alone does not cure an authority gap.**


        </details>

    available_excerpt: "# Harbor 2 — Matter dossier\n\n*Current as of September 12,
      2026*\n\n## Current position\n\nThe Harbor Pay acquisition migration needs legal
      and compliance review before close.\n\nMosaic plans to move 240,000 consumer
      accounts and 12,000 business accounts into its app on day one after an asset
      purchase. The plan also assumes Harbor’s licenses can support four new states,
      relies on Harbor’s prior identity checks, changes the transaction-monitoring
      system at close, and starts a $50 welcome offer one day later.\n\nThe main obstacle
      is the lack of confirmed authority for Mosaic to provide regulated services
      in the four Harbor-only states. The current licensing research retrieved four
      sources but did not read or verify the controlling passages. The identity-check
      records, open alerts, suspicious activity report history, and monitoring transition
      also remain unresolved.\n\nThe acquisition can close separately from a full
      migration. However, the saved working view is that Mosaic should not launch
      a regulated service in a state without confirmed authority. No final launch
      decision has been recorded.\n\nThe reported close date is Nov 15. Separate matter
      metadata lists `2026-09-11` as a target or requested launch date. The records
      do not explain this difference.\n\n## Decision question\n\nDoes any part of
      the Harbor Pay acquisition migration need legal review before the Nov 15 close?\n\n##
      Issues\n\n<!-- issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-94e42e2b38090045 -->\n### Money transmission
      licensing: whether Mosaic Relay can operate under the combined 44-state footprint
      and whether Harbor's MTLs transfer/survive the acquisition\n\n**Current answer**\n\nYes.
      This is a pre-close legal review item and a launch gate for the four Harbor-only
      states. Mosaic should not treat Harbor’s money transmitter licenses as assets
      that automatically move to Mosaic in the asset purchase.\n\n**Rule and support**\n\nThe
      saved research position is that the answer must be determined state by state.
      It may depend on the licensed entity, transaction structure, actual funds flow,
      required filings, regulator approval, and whether Harbor remains the provider.\n\nThe
      research retrieved four sources, but no passages were read. It therefore does
      not yet verify an applicable state rule, approval path, or regulator position.
      See the [licensing research packet](03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/RES-20260911-0a5f07.md).\n\n**Application**\n\nHarbor
      reportedly holds licenses in 38 states. Mosaic reportedly holds licenses in
      41 states. The business plans to use or assume Harbor’s licenses in four additional
      markets. The transaction is an asset purchase, and the migration would give
      users one Mosaic account and access to Mosaic’s products.\n\nThese facts do
      not establish that Harbor’s licenses authorize Mosaic after close. A bank-held-funds
      structure remains only a hypothetical. It could change the analysis if the bank
      has the legally important customer, account, transfer, settlement, and repayment
      roles. Custody by a bank alone does not answer the question.\n\n**Next step**\n\nInformation
      and verified legal analysis remain open. Identify the four Harbor-only states.
      For each state, document Mosaic’s authority, required transaction filings or
      approvals, any permitted interim operation, the planned funds flow, Harbor’s
      outstanding obligations, and the conditions for license surrender. Regulated
      launch should remain excluded in a state until a lawful route is confirmed.\n\n<!--
      issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-ca95e529feeb1045 -->\n### BSA/AML and KYC: whether relying
      on Harbor's prior KYC (with missing underlying documents for pre-2023 users)
      satisfies Mosaic Relay's independent CIP/AML obligations\n\n**Current answer**\n\nLegal
      and compliance review is required before Mosaic relies on Harbor’s prior identity
      checks without re-verification. This issue has not been researched.\n\n**Rule
      and support**\n\nThe saved record identifies Mosaic’s Bank Secrecy Act and anti-money-laundering
      program, including its customer identification program, as the relevant framework.
      No external legal authority has been saved for this issue.\n\nMosaic’s own program
      requirements are also unknown. Compliance has not confirmed whether the program
      permits reliance on another institution’s prior checks.\n\n**Application**\n\nHarbor’s
      former vendor produced pass/fail results but shut down in 2024. Mosaic does
      not have the underlying documents for users onboarded before 2023. The planned
      migration would give Harbor users access to Mosaic’s full product set without
      new verification.\n\nA pass result does not, by itself, establish that Mosaic
      has the records or legal basis required by its own program. The effect could
      include re-verification, limits on account access, or another documented control.
      The saved material does not determine which response is required.\n\n**Next
      step**\n\nResearch, information, and implementation remain open. Compliance
      must review Mosaic’s written program and the available Harbor records. Legal
      must then determine whether the proposed reliance is permitted and what treatment
      is required for users whose underlying records are missing.\n\n<!-- issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-69af3f282d21ef38
      -->\n### SAR and alert handling: obligations to review, file, and manage Harbor's
      ~40 open alerts and SARs, and successor liability\n\n**Current answer**\n\nThe
      alerts and suspicious activity report history require review before migration.
      Their effect on the transaction and post-close duties cannot be assessed while
      they remain unread. This issue has not been researched.\n\n**Rule and support**\n\nThe
      saved analysis identifies federal financial-crime duties and possible successor
      obligations as relevant. No external legal authority has been saved for this
      issue.\n\n**Application**\n\nDiligence found about 40 open alerts and a handful
      of suspicious activity reports from the prior year. None have been reviewed.
      The records therefore do not show the nature or age of the alerts, whether investigations
      are complete, whether filing deadlines may apply, or whether the matters concern
      users scheduled for migration.\n\nAn asset purchase does not establish that
      all duties disappear or transfer. The purchase documents, Harbor’s retained
      duties, Mosaic’s post-close role, and the contents of the files may affect the
      answer.\n\n**Next step**\n\nInformation, research, and a responsibility decision
      remain open. Review the alerts and report history. Create a case list showing
      status, age, required action, responsible entity, records to transfer, and treatment
      after close. Escalate any matter that may affect the deal or migration population.\n\n<!--
      issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-4825f0af7ec75542 -->\n### Monitoring vendor transition:
      continuity of transaction monitoring when the SaaS contract terminates at close\n\n**Current
      answer**\n\nThe day-one system change needs compliance review and operational
      testing before migration. The saved record does not establish that monitoring
      will continue without a gap. This issue has not been researched.\n\n**Rule and
      support**\n\nThe relevant saved framework is continuity of Mosaic’s financial-crime
      monitoring controls. No external legal authority has been saved for this issue.\n\n**Application**\n\nHarbor’s
      monitoring software contract ends at close. The plan is to redirect Harbor’s
      transaction feed to Mosaic’s system on day one. No saved evidence shows that
      the data mapping, alert rules, historical data, system capacity, or failure
      process has been tested.\n\nThis creates a possible monitoring gap at the same
      time that 252,000 accounts move into Mosaic’s app. The open Harbor alerts make
      continuity more important because the new system may need historical context
      and unresolved case data.\n\n**Next step**\n\nEvidence and implementation remain
      open. Complete an end-to-end test that covers all Harbor transaction types,
      data fields, alert generation, case transfer, historical access, reconciliation,
      and failure handling. Document the result and any temporary control needed at
      close.\n\n<!-- issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-72810d0a43d3ce66 -->\n### Marketing compliance:
      the $50 welcome bonus offer and any state money transmission or consumer protection
      implications\n\n**Current answer**\n\nThe offer needs legal review before release.
      The saved material does not contain enough information to determine the required
      disclosures or other limits. This issue has not been researched.\n\n**Rule and
      support**\n\nState money-transmission and consumer-protection rules may apply
      based on the offer’s terms, funds flow, eligibility limits, and advertising
      claims. No external legal authority has been saved for this issue.\n\n**Application**\n\nMarketing
      plans a $50 bonus on a customer’s first transfer, with launch at close plus
      one day. The offer structure remains unknown. The record does not state who
      funds the bonus, when it becomes payable, whether it can be withdrawn, what
      transfer qualifies, which users or states are eligible, or whether the offer
      expires.\n\nThe campaign also depends on the underlying service being lawfully
      available. Marketing approval cannot solve an unresolved license or migration
      restriction.\n\n**Next step**\n\nInformation and legal analysis remain open.
      Obtain the complete offer terms, customer messages, eligibility rules, funds
      flow, state scope, and complaint process. Review these items before publication
      and exclude any state or customer group that cannot use the underlying service.\n\n##
      Assumptions and record limits\n\nNo additional assumptions are treated as confirmed
      facts.\n\nThe saved record still lists open model assumptions about license
      transfer, KYC sufficiency, and monitoring continuity. This dossier does not
      rely on those assumptions as true. Each is an unresolved matter requiring evidence.\n\nThe
      acquisition structure is now reported as an asset purchase. The older assumption
      that the structure was unknown is stale. The bank-held-funds structure remains
      hypothetical and does not change the current direction.\n\n## Next actions\n\nNo
      open action has a recorded owner or due date. The entries below are proposed
      actions, not assigned work.\n\n1. **Proposed — Review Harbor’s open alerts and
      suspicious activity report history.**  \n   **Owner:** Not assigned.  \n   **Date:**
      No date recorded.  \n   **Evidence needed:** Case files, filing history, alert
      age and status, investigation notes, deadlines, user links, and a responsibility
      list for pre- and post-close work.\n\n2. **Proposed — Complete the four-state
      licensing matrix and set launch gates.**  \n   **Owner:** Not assigned.  \n
      \  **Date:** No date recorded.  \n   **Evidence needed:** Identity of the four
      states, current licenses, state rules and regulator guidance, required filings
      or approvals, funds-flow documents, purchase terms, outstanding obligations,
      and surrender requirements.\n\n3. **Proposed — Decide how Mosaic will satisfy
      identity-check and recordkeeping duties.**  \n   **Owner:** Not assigned.  \n
      \  **Date:** No date recorded.  \n   **Evidence needed:** Mosaic’s written anti-money-laundering
      and customer identification programs, Harbor’s procedures, available customer
      records, vendor outputs, population counts, and a proposed treatment for missing
      records.\n\n4. **Proposed — Test transaction-monitoring continuity.**  \n   **Owner:**
      Not assigned.  \n   **Date:** No date recorded.  \n   **Evidence needed:** Test
      results, data mapping, rule coverage, case migration, historical access, reconciliation,
      capacity results, and a failure or rollback plan.\n\n5. **Proposed — Review
      the $50 welcome offer before release.**  \n   **Owner:** Not assigned.  \n   **Date:**
      No date recorded.  \n   **Evidence needed:** Final terms, advertisements, eligibility
      rules, qualifying-transfer definition, state scope, bonus funds flow, withdrawal
      limits, expiration terms, and complaint process.\n\nThe only saved work item,
      **Orient to the request**, was assigned to the Lawyer and completed on September
      9, 2026. See [WI-20260909-dc797b](03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/work-items/WI-20260909-dc797b.md).\n\n##
      Current work product and records\n\n- **Current work product:** No current draft
      or final path is recorded.\n- **Licensing research:** [RES-20260911-0a5f07.md](03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/RES-20260911-0a5f07.md)\n-
      **Reported facts:** [facts.md](03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/facts.md)\n- **Original
      request:** [request.md](03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/request.md)\n- **Prior dossier
      revision:** [DOS-c993ab2326efd727cc3d.md](03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/dossier-revisions/DOS-c993ab2326efd727cc3d.md)\n\n<!--
      saved-issue-analysis:start -->\n## Saved issue answers\n\n<!-- issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-94e42e2b38090045
      -->\n### Money transmission licensing: whether Mosaic Relay can operate under
      the combined 44-state footprint and whether Harbor's MTLs transfer/survive the
      acquisition\n\nDo not rely on transferability without an applicable state rule,
      approval, or written regulator position.\n\nThe answer must be determined state
      by state and must cover more than technical change-of-control rules.\n\nThis
      path is possible only if Harbor remains the real provider and the relevant states
      permit the arrangement. The unified Mosaic account design may conflict with
      that position.\n\nThe result depends on the contracts and actual funds flow.
      Custody alone should not be treated as a licensing exemption or transfer mechanism.\n\nResearch
      saved. 4 sources retrieved; 0 with passages read.\n\nNext step: Identify the
      four Harbor-only states and complete one matrix covering license authority,
      transaction filings, interim operation, bank custody, outstanding obligations,
      and license surrender.\n\n<details>\n<summary>Detailed analysis</summary>\n\n**Practical
      answer**\n\n**Do not treat Harbor’s money transmitter licenses as assets that
      move to Mosaic in the asset purchase.** Use this closing rule:\n\n> **No confirmed
      authority means no regulated launch by Mosaic in that state.**\n\nThe asset
      purchase can still close. However, Mosaic should not activate regulated services
      in the four Harbor-only states unless one of these conditions is met:\n\n1.
      Mosaic obtains its own license.\n2. The state approves a transfer or other license
      change.\n3. The regulator confirms a lawful interim structure.\n4. Harbor remains
      the real licensed provider under a valid transition plan.\n5. Mosaic blocks
      regulated functions in that state.\n\nThe planned unified Mosaic account is
      important. It suggests that Mosaic, not Harbor, will become the service provider.
      If that is correct, keeping Harbor’s legal entity and licenses alive might not
      give Mosaic authority to operate.\n\n**No verified operative authority retrieved.**
      The collection found the CSBS Model Money Transmission Modernization Act and
      a New York regulator licensing page. However, no exact provision about an asset
      sale, license transfer, interim operation, or custody was available for review.
      Also, a model act is not proof of the law adopted in a specific state. The state-specific
      conclusions below remain conditional.\n\n**Work needed before launch**\n\n|
      Work | Why | Proposed owner | Needed by | Evidence to proceed | Fallback |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n|
      Identify the four Harbor-only states | These states have the clearest authority
      gap | Licensing team | Now | Confirmed license list and covered activities |
      Block regulated functions in those states |\n| Build a matrix for every affected
      state | Transfer, notice, approval, and surrender rules can differ | State regulatory
      counsel | Before the closing conditions are final | Applicable rule, filing
      deadline, regulator response, and license status | Delay migration in the affected
      state |\n| Confirm the post-close service provider | The licensed entity must
      match the actual operating model | Product and deal counsel | Before customer
      notices and account design are final | Contracts, user interface, funds-flow
      diagram, and operating roles | Keep Harbor as the actual provider where permitted
      |\n| Review notices and approvals | An asset sale can create separate seller
      and buyer requirements | Licensing team | Before each state deadline | Filing
      receipts and required approvals or non-objections | Make approval a state launch
      condition |\n| Confirm interim operating authority | A pending application should
      not be assumed to permit operation | State regulatory counsel | Before close
      | Applicable rule or written regulator confirmation | Keep Harbor customers
      segregated or restrict transactions |\n| Review partner-bank arrangements |
      Bank custody does not, by itself, establish Mosaic’s licensing position | Payments
      counsel and bank relationship owner | Before balances move | Bank agreements,
      account titles, control rights, reconciliation design, and bank consent | Keep
      funds and obligations in the existing structure |\n| Prepare license run-off
      and surrender plans | Harbor may retain duties for balances, claims, records,
      and reports | Harbor compliance and deal counsel | Before surrender or operational
      shutdown | Regulator-approved plan and continued bonds and financial coverage
      | Keep Harbor licensed and operational longer |\n\n**State notice and approval**\n\nDo
      not search only for a rule called “license transfer.” Review each state for:\n\n-
      sale of substantially all assets;\n- change of control;\n- change in business
      plan;\n- change in licensed activities;\n- assignment of outstanding payment
      obligations;\n- change in responsible persons, locations, or agents;\n- license
      surrender or termination;\n- customer agreement assignment; and\n- movement
      of stored balances or customer funds.\n\nAn asset purchase might fall outside
      a technical “change of control” rule because Harbor’s ownership does not change.
      That does not resolve the issue. Harbor might still need a notice or surrender
      approval. Mosaic might separately need authority before it starts the regulated
      activity.\n\nThe state matrix should distinguish:\n\n- notice only;\n- approval
      before closing;\n- approval before customer migration;\n- new license required;\n-
      operation permitted while an application is pending;\n- regulator-approved transition
      available; and\n- no supported interim path.\n\n**Interim operation**\n\nHarbor
      could remain the licensed provider for a transition period. However, the structure
      must be real, not only contractual language.\n\nA supportable structure would
      normally need to show that:\n\n- Harbor remains responsible to customers.\n-
      Customer terms name Harbor as the provider.\n- Harbor controls the regulated
      activity as required.\n- Harbor’s licenses, bonds, and financial coverage remain
      active.\n- Customer funds and outstanding obligations remain covered.\n- Harbor
      can complete reports, refunds, complaints, and examinations.\n- Required records
      remain available.\n- Mosaic’s operational role is permitted.\n- The partner
      bank accepts the structure.\n- Each relevant regulator permits or accepts the
      structure.\n\nThe present plan creates tension with this approach. A single
      Mosaic account, one combined balance, and full access to Mosaic products make
      it harder to show that Harbor remains the true provider.\n\n**Partner-bank custody**\n\nA
      partner bank account is not automatically a substitute for a money transmitter
      license. The key questions concern the complete activity and obligation flow.\n\nFor
      each customer balance and transfer, document:\n\n1. Who receives the customer’s
      instruction?\n2. Who accepts the obligation to transmit or return the funds?\n3.
      In whose name is the bank account held?\n4. Is it a custodial or “for benefit
      of” account?\n5. Who can direct withdrawals?\n6. Who owns the ledger and resolves
      errors?\n7. Who bears loss if a transfer fails?\n8. Which entity owes the customer
      a refund?\n9. Which entity’s license, bond, and permissible investments cover
      the obligation?\n10. Does the bank agreement permit assignment, migration, or
      a change in the program manager?\n11. Is bank consent required before closing
      or migration?\n12. What happens to funds if Harbor, Mosaic, or the bank relationship
      ends?\n\nA bank-led model might provide a different licensing analysis if the
      bank is the actual provider and Mosaic performs only permitted services. A bank
      account that merely holds funds for Mosaic or Harbor is different. The legal
      documents, customer terms, control rights, and actual operations must all support
      the claimed model.\n\nAlso review:\n\n- account segregation;\n- daily reconciliation;\n-
      shortfalls and reserve funding;\n- bank setoff rights;\n- liens and security
      interests;\n- continuity if the account is frozen;\n- treatment of pending transfers;\n-
      return and chargeback duties;\n- insolvency treatment;\n- customer disclosures;
      and\n- whether any deposit-insurance statement is accurate for the actual account
      structure.\n\n**Recommended closing position**\n\nKeep the acquisition closing
      separate from the regulated-service launch.\n\nFor the four Harbor-only states,
      use a state launch hold unless Mosaic has documented authority. For overlapping
      states, confirm that Mosaic’s existing licenses cover the complete post-close
      product set and funds flow.\n\nDo not surrender Harbor’s licenses or release
      its bonds until the applicable regulators permit it and all outstanding obligations
      have been assigned, completed, or placed into an approved run-off structure.\n\nThis
      investigation does not change the current recommendation. It adds one material
      point: **partner-bank custody is part of the licensing analysis, but custody
      alone does not cure an authority gap.**\n\n</details>\n\nResearch history: [Saved
      research 1](03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/RES-20260911-0a5f07.md)\n\n<!--
      issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-ca95e529feeb1045 -->\n### BSA/AML and KYC: whether relying
      on Harbor's prior KYC (with missing underlying documents for pre-2023 users)
      satisfies Mosaic Relay's independent CIP/AML obligations\n\nNo separate current
      answer is saved for this issue.\n\nInitial answer — research not complete.\n\n<!--
      issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-69af3f282d21ef38 -->\n### SAR and alert handling: obligations
      to review, file, and manage Harbor's ~40 open alerts and SARs, and successor
      liability\n\nNo separate current answer is saved for this issue.\n\nInitial
      answer — research not complete.\n\n<!-- issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-4825f0af7ec75542
      -->\n### Monitoring vendor transition: continuity of transaction monitoring
      when the SaaS contract terminates at close\n\nNo separate current answer is
      saved for this issue.\n\nInitial answer — research not complete.\n\n<!-- issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-72810d0a43d3ce66
      -->\n### Marketing compliance: the $50 welcome bonus offer and any state money
      transmission or consumer protection implications\n\nNo separate current answer
      is saved for this issue.\n\nInitial answer — research not complete.\n<!-- saved-issue-analysis:end
      -->\n\nDossier preview generated; no matter records changed.\n\nIncluded full
      saved answers for 5 issue(s)."
    record_revision: 6520a61b516490d9136171066a2729112f489ec8044ef6d51b678e81af40bf67
    source_version: 6520a61b516490d9136171066a2729112f489ec8044ef6d51b678e81af40bf67
    support_state: supplied
    captured_record:
      message_id: MSG-20260912-5adaa5
      content: "# Harbor 2 — Matter dossier\n\n*Current as of September 12, 2026*\n\n##
        Current position\n\nThe Harbor Pay acquisition migration needs legal and compliance
        review before close.\n\nMosaic plans to move 240,000 consumer accounts and
        12,000 business accounts into its app on day one after an asset purchase.
        The plan also assumes Harbor’s licenses can support four new states, relies
        on Harbor’s prior identity checks, changes the transaction-monitoring system
        at close, and starts a $50 welcome offer one day later.\n\nThe main obstacle
        is the lack of confirmed authority for Mosaic to provide regulated services
        in the four Harbor-only states. The current licensing research retrieved four
        sources but did not read or verify the controlling passages. The identity-check
        records, open alerts, suspicious activity report history, and monitoring transition
        also remain unresolved.\n\nThe acquisition can close separately from a full
        migration. However, the saved working view is that Mosaic should not launch
        a regulated service in a state without confirmed authority. No final launch
        decision has been recorded.\n\nThe reported close date is Nov 15. Separate
        matter metadata lists `2026-09-11` as a target or requested launch date. The
        records do not explain this difference.\n\n## Decision question\n\nDoes any
        part of the Harbor Pay acquisition migration need legal review before the
        Nov 15 close?\n\n## Issues\n\n<!-- issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-94e42e2b38090045 -->\n###
        Money transmission licensing: whether Mosaic Relay can operate under the combined
        44-state footprint and whether Harbor's MTLs transfer/survive the acquisition\n\n**Current
        answer**\n\nYes. This is a pre-close legal review item and a launch gate for
        the four Harbor-only states. Mosaic should not treat Harbor’s money transmitter
        licenses as assets that automatically move to Mosaic in the asset purchase.\n\n**Rule
        and support**\n\nThe saved research position is that the answer must be determined
        state by state. It may depend on the licensed entity, transaction structure,
        actual funds flow, required filings, regulator approval, and whether Harbor
        remains the provider.\n\nThe research retrieved four sources, but no passages
        were read. It therefore does not yet verify an applicable state rule, approval
        path, or regulator position. See the [licensing research packet](03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/RES-20260911-0a5f07.md).\n\n**Application**\n\nHarbor
        reportedly holds licenses in 38 states. Mosaic reportedly holds licenses in
        41 states. The business plans to use or assume Harbor’s licenses in four additional
        markets. The transaction is an asset purchase, and the migration would give
        users one Mosaic account and access to Mosaic’s products.\n\nThese facts do
        not establish that Harbor’s licenses authorize Mosaic after close. A bank-held-funds
        structure remains only a hypothetical. It could change the analysis if the
        bank has the legally important customer, account, transfer, settlement, and
        repayment roles. Custody by a bank alone does not answer the question.\n\n**Next
        step**\n\nInformation and verified legal analysis remain open. Identify the
        four Harbor-only states. For each state, document Mosaic’s authority, required
        transaction filings or approvals, any permitted interim operation, the planned
        funds flow, Harbor’s outstanding obligations, and the conditions for license
        surrender. Regulated launch should remain excluded in a state until a lawful
        route is confirmed.\n\n<!-- issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-ca95e529feeb1045 -->\n###
        BSA/AML and KYC: whether relying on Harbor's prior KYC (with missing underlying
        documents for pre-2023 users) satisfies Mosaic Relay's independent CIP/AML
        obligations\n\n**Current answer**\n\nLegal and compliance review is required
        before Mosaic relies on Harbor’s prior identity checks without re-verification.
        This issue has not been researched.\n\n**Rule and support**\n\nThe saved record
        identifies Mosaic’s Bank Secrecy Act and anti-money-laundering program, including
        its customer identification program, as the relevant framework. No external
        legal authority has been saved for this issue.\n\nMosaic’s own program requirements
        are also unknown. Compliance has not confirmed whether the program permits
        reliance on another institution’s prior checks.\n\n**Application**\n\nHarbor’s
        former vendor produced pass/fail results but shut down in 2024. Mosaic does
        not have the underlying documents for users onboarded before 2023. The planned
        migration would give Harbor users access to Mosaic’s full product set without
        new verification.\n\nA pass result does not, by itself, establish that Mosaic
        has the records or legal basis required by its own program. The effect could
        include re-verification, limits on account access, or another documented control.
        The saved material does not determine which response is required.\n\n**Next
        step**\n\nResearch, information, and implementation remain open. Compliance
        must review Mosaic’s written program and the available Harbor records. Legal
        must then determine whether the proposed reliance is permitted and what treatment
        is required for users whose underlying records are missing.\n\n<!-- issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-69af3f282d21ef38
        -->\n### SAR and alert handling: obligations to review, file, and manage Harbor's
        ~40 open alerts and SARs, and successor liability\n\n**Current answer**\n\nThe
        alerts and suspicious activity report history require review before migration.
        Their effect on the transaction and post-close duties cannot be assessed while
        they remain unread. This issue has not been researched.\n\n**Rule and support**\n\nThe
        saved analysis identifies federal financial-crime duties and possible successor
        obligations as relevant. No external legal authority has been saved for this
        issue.\n\n**Application**\n\nDiligence found about 40 open alerts and a handful
        of suspicious activity reports from the prior year. None have been reviewed.
        The records therefore do not show the nature or age of the alerts, whether
        investigations are complete, whether filing deadlines may apply, or whether
        the matters concern users scheduled for migration.\n\nAn asset purchase does
        not establish that all duties disappear or transfer. The purchase documents,
        Harbor’s retained duties, Mosaic’s post-close role, and the contents of the
        files may affect the answer.\n\n**Next step**\n\nInformation, research, and
        a responsibility decision remain open. Review the alerts and report history.
        Create a case list showing status, age, required action, responsible entity,
        records to transfer, and treatment after close. Escalate any matter that may
        affect the deal or migration population.\n\n<!-- issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-4825f0af7ec75542
        -->\n### Monitoring vendor transition: continuity of transaction monitoring
        when the SaaS contract terminates at close\n\n**Current answer**\n\nThe day-one
        system change needs compliance review and operational testing before migration.
        The saved record does not establish that monitoring will continue without
        a gap. This issue has not been researched.\n\n**Rule and support**\n\nThe
        relevant saved framework is continuity of Mosaic’s financial-crime monitoring
        controls. No external legal authority has been saved for this issue.\n\n**Application**\n\nHarbor’s
        monitoring software contract ends at close. The plan is to redirect Harbor’s
        transaction feed to Mosaic’s system on day one. No saved evidence shows that
        the data mapping, alert rules, historical data, system capacity, or failure
        process has been tested.\n\nThis creates a possible monitoring gap at the
        same time that 252,000 accounts move into Mosaic’s app. The open Harbor alerts
        make continuity more important because the new system may need historical
        context and unresolved case data.\n\n**Next step**\n\nEvidence and implementation
        remain open. Complete an end-to-end test that covers all Harbor transaction
        types, data fields, alert generation, case transfer, historical access, reconciliation,
        and failure handling. Document the result and any temporary control needed
        at close.\n\n<!-- issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-72810d0a43d3ce66 -->\n### Marketing
        compliance: the $50 welcome bonus offer and any state money transmission or
        consumer protection implications\n\n**Current answer**\n\nThe offer needs
        legal review before release. The saved material does not contain enough information
        to determine the required disclosures or other limits. This issue has not
        been researched.\n\n**Rule and support**\n\nState money-transmission and consumer-protection
        rules may apply based on the offer’s terms, funds flow, eligibility limits,
        and advertising claims. No external legal authority has been saved for this
        issue.\n\n**Application**\n\nMarketing plans a $50 bonus on a customer’s first
        transfer, with launch at close plus one day. The offer structure remains unknown.
        The record does not state who funds the bonus, when it becomes payable, whether
        it can be withdrawn, what transfer qualifies, which users or states are eligible,
        or whether the offer expires.\n\nThe campaign also depends on the underlying
        service being lawfully available. Marketing approval cannot solve an unresolved
        license or migration restriction.\n\n**Next step**\n\nInformation and legal
        analysis remain open. Obtain the complete offer terms, customer messages,
        eligibility rules, funds flow, state scope, and complaint process. Review
        these items before publication and exclude any state or customer group that
        cannot use the underlying service.\n\n## Assumptions and record limits\n\nNo
        additional assumptions are treated as confirmed facts.\n\nThe saved record
        still lists open model assumptions about license transfer, KYC sufficiency,
        and monitoring continuity. This dossier does not rely on those assumptions
        as true. Each is an unresolved matter requiring evidence.\n\nThe acquisition
        structure is now reported as an asset purchase. The older assumption that
        the structure was unknown is stale. The bank-held-funds structure remains
        hypothetical and does not change the current direction.\n\n## Next actions\n\nNo
        open action has a recorded owner or due date. The entries below are proposed
        actions, not assigned work.\n\n1. **Proposed — Review Harbor’s open alerts
        and suspicious activity report history.**  \n   **Owner:** Not assigned.  \n
        \  **Date:** No date recorded.  \n   **Evidence needed:** Case files, filing
        history, alert age and status, investigation notes, deadlines, user links,
        and a responsibility list for pre- and post-close work.\n\n2. **Proposed —
        Complete the four-state licensing matrix and set launch gates.**  \n   **Owner:**
        Not assigned.  \n   **Date:** No date recorded.  \n   **Evidence needed:**
        Identity of the four states, current licenses, state rules and regulator guidance,
        required filings or approvals, funds-flow documents, purchase terms, outstanding
        obligations, and surrender requirements.\n\n3. **Proposed — Decide how Mosaic
        will satisfy identity-check and recordkeeping duties.**  \n   **Owner:** Not
        assigned.  \n   **Date:** No date recorded.  \n   **Evidence needed:** Mosaic’s
        written anti-money-laundering and customer identification programs, Harbor’s
        procedures, available customer records, vendor outputs, population counts,
        and a proposed treatment for missing records.\n\n4. **Proposed — Test transaction-monitoring
        continuity.**  \n   **Owner:** Not assigned.  \n   **Date:** No date recorded.
        \ \n   **Evidence needed:** Test results, data mapping, rule coverage, case
        migration, historical access, reconciliation, capacity results, and a failure
        or rollback plan.\n\n5. **Proposed — Review the $50 welcome offer before release.**
        \ \n   **Owner:** Not assigned.  \n   **Date:** No date recorded.  \n   **Evidence
        needed:** Final terms, advertisements, eligibility rules, qualifying-transfer
        definition, state scope, bonus funds flow, withdrawal limits, expiration terms,
        and complaint process.\n\nThe only saved work item, **Orient to the request**,
        was assigned to the Lawyer and completed on September 9, 2026. See [WI-20260909-dc797b](03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/work-items/WI-20260909-dc797b.md).\n\n##
        Current work product and records\n\n- **Current work product:** No current
        draft or final path is recorded.\n- **Licensing research:** [RES-20260911-0a5f07.md](03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/RES-20260911-0a5f07.md)\n-
        **Reported facts:** [facts.md](03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/facts.md)\n- **Original
        request:** [request.md](03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/request.md)\n- **Prior
        dossier revision:** [DOS-c993ab2326efd727cc3d.md](03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/dossier-revisions/DOS-c993ab2326efd727cc3d.md)\n\n<!--
        saved-issue-analysis:start -->\n## Saved issue answers\n\n<!-- issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-94e42e2b38090045

        the combined 44-state footprint and whether Harbor's MTLs transfer/survive

        rule, approval, or written regulator position.\n\nThe answer must be determined
        state by state and must cover more than technical change-of-control rules.\n\nThis
        path is possible only if Harbor remains the real provider and the relevant
        states permit the arrangement. The unified Mosaic account design may conflict
        with that position.\n\nThe result depends on the contracts and actual funds
        flow. Custody alone should not be treated as a licensing exemption or transfer
        mechanism.\n\nResearch saved. 4 sources retrieved; 0 with passages read.\n\nNext
        step: Identify the four Harbor-only states and complete one matrix covering
        license authority, transaction filings, interim operation, bank custody, outstanding
        obligations, and license surrender.\n\n<details>\n<summary>Detailed analysis</summary>\n\n**Practical
        answer**\n\n**Do not treat Harbor’s money transmitter licenses as assets that

        Harbor remains the real licensed provider under a valid transition plan.\n5.
        Mosaic blocks regulated functions in that state.\n\nThe planned unified Mosaic
        account is important. It suggests that Mosaic, not Harbor, will become the

        alive might not give Mosaic authority to operate.\n\n**No verified operative

        provision about an asset sale, license transfer, interim operation, or custody
        was available for review. Also, a model act is not proof of the law adopted
        in a specific state. The state-specific conclusions below remain conditional.\n\n**Work
        needed before launch**\n\n| Work | Why | Proposed owner | Needed by | Evidence
        to proceed | Fallback |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| Identify the four Harbor-only
        states | These states have the clearest authority gap | Licensing team | Now
        | Confirmed license list and covered activities | Block regulated functions
        in those states |\n| Build a matrix for every affected state | Transfer, notice,
        approval, and surrender rules can differ | State regulatory counsel | Before
        the closing conditions are final | Applicable rule, filing deadline, regulator
        response, and license status | Delay migration in the affected state |\n|
        Confirm the post-close service provider | The licensed entity must match the

        and account design are final | Contracts, user interface, funds-flow diagram,
        and operating roles | Keep Harbor as the actual provider where permitted |\n|
        Review notices and approvals | An asset sale can create separate seller and

        close | Applicable rule or written regulator confirmation | Keep Harbor customers

        | Payments counsel and bank relationship owner | Before balances move | Bank
        agreements, account titles, control rights, reconciliation design, and bank
        consent | Keep funds and obligations in the existing structure |\n| Prepare
        license run-off and surrender plans | Harbor may retain duties for balances,
        claims, records, and reports | Harbor compliance and deal counsel | Before
        surrender or operational shutdown | Regulator-approved plan and continued
        bonds and financial coverage | Keep Harbor licensed and operational longer
        |\n\n**State notice and approval**\n\nDo not search only for a rule called
        “license transfer.” Review each state for:\n\n- sale of substantially all
        assets;\n- change of control;\n- change in business plan;\n- change in licensed
        activities;\n- assignment of outstanding payment obligations;\n- change in
        responsible persons, locations, or agents;\n- license surrender or termination;\n-
        customer agreement assignment; and\n- movement of stored balances or customer
        funds.\n\nAn asset purchase might fall outside a technical “change of control”
        rule because Harbor’s ownership does not change. That does not resolve the
        issue. Harbor might still need a notice or surrender approval. Mosaic might
        separately need authority before it starts the regulated activity.\n\nThe
        state matrix should distinguish:\n\n- notice only;\n- approval before closing;\n-
        approval before customer migration;\n- new license required;\n- operation
        permitted while an application is pending;\n- regulator-approved transition
        available; and\n- no supported interim path.\n\n**Interim operation**\n\nHarbor
        could remain the licensed provider for a transition period. However, the structure
        must be real, not only contractual language.\n\nA supportable structure would
        normally need to show that:\n\n- Harbor remains responsible to customers.\n-
        Customer terms name Harbor as the provider.\n- Harbor controls the regulated
        activity as required.\n- Harbor’s licenses, bonds, and financial coverage
        remain active.\n- Customer funds and outstanding obligations remain covered.\n-
        Harbor can complete reports, refunds, complaints, and examinations.\n- Required
        records remain available.\n- Mosaic’s operational role is permitted.\n- The
        partner bank accepts the structure.\n- Each relevant regulator permits or
        accepts the structure.\n\nThe present plan creates tension with this approach.
        A single Mosaic account, one combined balance, and full access to Mosaic products
        make it harder to show that Harbor remains the true provider.\n\n**Partner-bank
        custody**\n\nA partner bank account is not automatically a substitute for
        a money transmitter license. The key questions concern the complete activity
        and obligation flow.\n\nFor each customer balance and transfer, document:\n\n1.
        Who receives the customer’s instruction?\n2. Who accepts the obligation to
        transmit or return the funds?\n3. In whose name is the bank account held?\n4.
        Is it a custodial or “for benefit of” account?\n5. Who can direct withdrawals?\n6.
        Who owns the ledger and resolves errors?\n7. Who bears loss if a transfer
        fails?\n8. Which entity owes the customer a refund?\n9. Which entity’s license,
        bond, and permissible investments cover the obligation?\n10. Does the bank
        agreement permit assignment, migration, or a change in the program manager?\n11.
        Is bank consent required before closing or migration?\n12. What happens to
        funds if Harbor, Mosaic, or the bank relationship ends?\n\nA bank-led model
        might provide a different licensing analysis if the bank is the actual provider
        and Mosaic performs only permitted services. A bank account that merely holds
        funds for Mosaic or Harbor is different. The legal documents, customer terms,
        control rights, and actual operations must all support the claimed model.\n\nAlso
        review:\n\n- account segregation;\n- daily reconciliation;\n- shortfalls and
        reserve funding;\n- bank setoff rights;\n- liens and security interests;\n-
        continuity if the account is frozen;\n- treatment of pending transfers;\n-
        return and chargeback duties;\n- insolvency treatment;\n- customer disclosures;
        and\n- whether any deposit-insurance statement is accurate for the actual
        account structure.\n\n**Recommended closing position**\n\nKeep the acquisition
        closing separate from the regulated-service launch.\n\nFor the four Harbor-only
        states, use a state launch hold unless Mosaic has documented authority. For
        overlapping states, confirm that Mosaic’s existing licenses cover the complete
        post-close product set and funds flow.\n\nDo not surrender Harbor’s licenses
        or release its bonds until the applicable regulators permit it and all outstanding
        obligations have been assigned, completed, or placed into an approved run-off
        structure.\n\nThis investigation does not change the current recommendation.
        It adds one material point: **partner-bank custody is part of the licensing
        analysis, but custody alone does not cure an authority gap.**\n\n</details>\n\nResearch
        history: [Saved research 1](03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/RES-20260911-0a5f07.md)\n\n<!--
        issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-ca95e529feeb1045 -->\n### BSA/AML and KYC: whether relying
        on Harbor's prior KYC (with missing underlying documents for pre-2023 users)
        satisfies Mosaic Relay's independent CIP/AML obligations\n\nNo separate current
        answer is saved for this issue.\n\nInitial answer — research not complete.\n\n<!--
        issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-69af3f282d21ef38 -->\n### SAR and alert handling: obligations
        to review, file, and manage Harbor's ~40 open alerts and SARs, and successor
        liability\n\nNo separate current answer is saved for this issue.\n\nInitial
        answer — research not complete.\n\n<!-- issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-4825f0af7ec75542
        -->\n### Monitoring vendor transition: continuity of transaction monitoring
        when the SaaS contract terminates at close\n\nNo separate current answer is
        saved for this issue.\n\nInitial answer — research not complete.\n\n<!-- issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-72810d0a43d3ce66
        -->\n### Marketing compliance: the $50 welcome bonus offer and any state money
        transmission or consumer protection implications\n\nNo separate current answer
        is saved for this issue.\n\nInitial answer — research not complete.\n<!--
        saved-issue-analysis:end -->\n\nDossier preview generated; no matter records
        changed.\n\nIncluded full saved answers for 5 issue(s)."
