    path: 03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/conversations/CONV-20260909-5299cf.md
    available_excerpt: "# Harbor 2 matter dossier\n\n## Current position\n\nThe Harbor
      Pay migration needs legal and compliance review before the targeted Nov 15 close.
      This is the accepted working view, not a recorded decision.\n\nMosaic plans
      to buy Harbor’s assets and move 240,000 consumer users and 12,000 business accounts
      into one Mosaic account on day one. The main obstacles are:\n\n1. Mosaic has
      not shown that it can use Harbor’s licenses in the four Harbor-only states.\n2.
      Mosaic has not confirmed that Harbor’s prior know-your-customer (KYC) results
      satisfy Mosaic’s anti-money-laundering (AML) program.\n3. Harbor’s approximately
      40 open alerts and recent suspicious activity report (SAR) history remain unread.\n4.
      The current monitoring service ends at close, and the replacement feed has not
      been shown to work.\n5. The terms of the $50 welcome bonus are not known.\n\nThe
      current direction remains unchanged. A full day-one migration is the planning
      baseline, but the saved working view supports launch restrictions where these
      conditions are not resolved. No decisions are recorded.\n\nThe analysis assumes
      that Harbor and Mosaic conduct regulated money-transmission activity and that
      Mosaic will become the operating provider immediately after close. It does not
      assume that Harbor’s licenses transfer, that Harbor’s KYC records are sufficient,
      or that the monitoring change will work without a gap.\n\nA newer reported answer
      confirms that the deal is an asset purchase. It replaces the earlier assumption
      that the deal structure was unknown.\n\n## Decision question\n\nDoes any part
      of the Harbor Pay acquisition migration need legal review before the Nov 15
      close?\n\n## Issues\n\n<!-- issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-94e42e2b38090045 -->\n### Money
      transmission licensing: whether Mosaic Relay can operate under the combined
      44-state footprint and whether Harbor's MTLs transfer/survive the acquisition\n\n**Current
      answer**\n\nMosaic should not rely on Harbor’s money transmitter licenses as
      assets that automatically move to Mosaic. Before Mosaic starts regulated services
      in a state, it needs documented authority for the complete post-close activity
      in that state.\n\nThe asset purchase may close separately from the service launch.
      For the four Harbor-only states, the current working position is to hold regulated
      functions unless Mosaic has its own license, required state approval, a supported
      interim structure, or another documented basis to operate.\n\n**Rule and support**\n\nThe
      saved research concludes that the answer must be determined state by state.
      The review must cover Mosaic’s authority, Harbor’s notices or approvals, outstanding
      obligations, interim operation, and license surrender.\n\nFour sources were
      retrieved, but no relevant passages were read or verified. The saved materials
      therefore do not establish an operative state rule:\n\n- [Research packet](03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/RES-20260911-0a5f07.md)\n-
      [CSBS Money Transmission Modernization Act — retrieved, passage not reviewed](03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/sources/SRC-37ee676da5b354ba8538-157583424427.md)\n-
      [New York Department of Financial Services licensing page — retrieved, passage
      not reviewed](03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/sources/SRC-71b20ecf65825d6522c4-fa80c95c5393.md)\n\nThe
      model act is not proof of the law in any specific state.\n\n**Application**\n\nHarbor
      reportedly holds licenses in 38 states. Mosaic reportedly holds licenses in
      41 states. The proposed combined footprint has 44 states and would open four
      new markets. Mosaic currently plans to use or assume Harbor’s licenses in those
      markets. The deal is an asset purchase.\n\nThe unified Mosaic account, combined
      balance, and access to Mosaic products suggest that Mosaic may become the actual
      provider. If so, keeping Harbor’s entity or licenses active might not give Mosaic
      authority. A transition in which Harbor remains the licensed provider could
      be possible only if the contracts, funds flow, customer terms, operating control,
      and applicable state requirements support that result.\n\nBank custody alone
      would not establish that Mosaic can perform the regulated activity. The full
      allocation of customer obligations and transfer control remains material.\n\n**Next
      step**\n\nIdentify the four Harbor-only states. Prepare one state matrix covering:\n\n-
      Mosaic’s authority for each post-close activity;\n- required transaction notices
      and approvals;\n- whether interim operation is permitted;\n- outstanding customer
      obligations;\n- bank custody and control;\n- Harbor’s run-off duties; and\n-
      license surrender requirements.\n\nDo not activate regulated functions in a
      state without documented authority.\n\n<!-- retained-issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-94e42e2b38090045:start
      -->\n<details>\n<summary>Saved research and prior analysis</summary>\n\nDo not
      rely on transferability without an applicable state rule, approval, or written
      regulator position.\n\nThe answer must be determined state by state and must
      cover more than technical change-of-control rules.\n\nThis path is possible
      only if Harbor remains the real provider and the relevant states permit the
      arrangement. The unified Mosaic account design may conflict with that position.\n\nThe
      result depends on the contracts and actual funds flow. Custody alone should
      not be treated as a licensing exemption or transfer mechanism.\n\nResearch saved.
      4 sources retrieved; 0 with passages read.\n\nNext step: Identify the four Harbor-only
      states and complete one matrix covering license authority, transaction filings,
      interim operation, bank custody, outstanding obligations, and license surrender.\n\n<details>\n<summary>Detailed
      analysis</summary>\n\n**Practical answer**\n\n**Do not treat Harbor’s money
      transmitter licenses as assets that move to Mosaic in the asset purchase.**
      Use this closing rule:\n\n> **No confirmed authority means no regulated launch
      by Mosaic in that state.**\n\nThe asset purchase can still close. However, Mosaic
      should not activate regulated services in the four Harbor-only states unless
      one of these conditions is met:\n\n1. Mosaic obtains its own license.\n2. The
      state approves a transfer or other license change.\n3. The regulator confirms
      a lawful interim structure.\n4. Harbor remains the real licensed provider under
      a valid transition plan.\n5. Mosaic blocks regulated functions in that state.\n\nThe
      planned unified Mosaic account is important. It suggests that Mosaic, not Harbor,
      will become the service provider. If that is correct, keeping Harbor’s legal
      entity and licenses alive might not give Mosaic authority to operate.\n\n**No
      verified operative authority retrieved.** The collection found the CSBS Model
      Money Transmission Modernization Act and a New York regulator licensing page.
      However, no exact provision about an asset sale, license transfer, interim operation,
      or custody was available for review. Also, a model act is not proof of the law
      adopted in a specific state. The state-specific conclusions below remain conditional.\n\n**Work
      needed before launch**\n\n| Work | Why | Proposed owner | Needed by | Evidence
      to proceed | Fallback |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| Identify the four Harbor-only
      states | These states have the clearest authority gap | Licensing team | Now
      | Confirmed license list and covered activities | Block regulated functions
      in those states |\n| Build a matrix for every affected state | Transfer, notice,
      approval, and surrender rules can differ | State regulatory counsel | Before
      the closing conditions are final | Applicable rule, filing deadline, regulator
      response, and license status | Delay migration in the affected state |\n| Confirm
      the post-close service provider | The licensed entity must match the actual
      operating model | Product and deal counsel | Before customer notices and account
      design are final | Contracts, user interface, funds-flow diagram, and operating
      roles | Keep Harbor as the actual provider where permitted |\n| Review notices
      and approvals | An asset sale can create separate seller and buyer requirements
      | Licensing team | Before each state deadline | Filing receipts and required
      approvals or non-objections | Make approval a state launch condition |\n| Confirm
      interim operating authority | A pending application should not be assumed to
      permit operation | State regulatory counsel | Before close | Applicable rule
      or written regulator confirmation | Keep Harbor customers segregated or restrict
      transactions |\n| Review partner-bank arrangements | Bank custody does not,
      by itself, establish Mosaic’s licensing position | Payments counsel and bank
      relationship owner | Before balances move | Bank agreements, account titles,
      control rights, reconciliation design, and bank consent | Keep funds and obligations
      in the existing structure |\n| Prepare license run-off and surrender plans |
      Harbor may retain duties for balances, claims, records, and reports | Harbor
      compliance and deal counsel | Before surrender or operational shutdown | Regulator-approved
      plan and continued bonds and financial coverage | Keep Harbor licensed and operational
      longer |\n\n**State notice and approval**\n\nDo not search only for a rule called
      “license transfer.” Review each state for:\n\n- sale of substantially all assets;\n-
      change of control;\n- change in business plan;\n- change in licensed activities;\n-
      assignment of outstanding payment obligations;\n- change in responsible persons,
      locations, or agents;\n- license surrender or termination;\n- customer agreement
      assignment; and\n- movement of stored balances or customer funds.\n\nAn asset
      purchase might fall outside a technical “change of control” rule because Harbor’s
      ownership does not change. That does not resolve the issue. Harbor might still
      need a notice or surrender approval. Mosaic might separately need authority
      before it starts the regulated activity.\n\nThe state matrix should distinguish:\n\n-
      notice only;\n- approval before closing;\n- approval before customer migration;\n-
      new license required;\n- operation permitted while an application is pending;\n-
      regulator-approved transition available; and\n- no supported interim path.\n\n**Interim
      operation**\n\nHarbor could remain the licensed provider for a transition period.
      However, the structure must be real, not only contractual language.\n\nA supportable
      structure would normally need to show that:\n\n- Harbor remains responsible
      to customers.\n- Customer terms name Harbor as the provider.\n- Harbor controls
      the regulated activity as required.\n- Harbor’s licenses, bonds, and financial
      coverage remain active.\n- Customer funds and outstanding obligations remain
      covered.\n- Harbor can complete reports, refunds, complaints, and examinations.\n-
      Required records remain available.\n- Mosaic’s operational role is permitted.\n-
      The partner bank accepts the structure.\n- Each relevant regulator permits or
      accepts the structure.\n\nThe present plan creates tension with this approach.
      A single Mosaic account, one combined balance, and full access to Mosaic products
      make it harder to show that Harbor remains the true provider.\n\n**Partner-bank
      custody**\n\nA partner bank account is not automatically a substitute for a
      money transmitter license. The key questions concern the complete activity and
      obligation flow.\n\nFor each customer balance and transfer, document:\n\n1.
      Who receives the customer’s instruction?\n2. Who accepts the obligation to transmit
      or return the funds?\n3. In whose name is the bank account held?\n4. Is it a
      custodial or “for benefit of” account?\n5. Who can direct withdrawals?\n6. Who
      owns the ledger and resolves errors?\n7. Who bears loss if a transfer fails?\n8.
      Which entity owes the customer a refund?\n9. Which entity’s license, bond, and
      permissible investments cover the obligation?\n10. Does the bank agreement permit
      assignment, migration, or a change in the program manager?\n11. Is bank consent
      required before closing or migration?\n12. What happens to funds if Harbor,
      Mosaic, or the bank relationship ends?\n\nA bank-led model might provide a different
      licensing analysis if the bank is the actual provider and Mosaic performs only
      permitted services. A bank account that merely holds funds for Mosaic or Harbor
      is different. The legal documents, customer terms, control rights, and actual
      operations must all support the claimed model.\n\nAlso review:\n\n- account
      segregation;\n- daily reconciliation;\n- shortfalls and reserve funding;\n-
      bank setoff rights;\n- liens and security interests;\n- continuity if the account
      is frozen;\n- treatment of pending transfers;\n- return and chargeback duties;\n-
      insolvency treatment;\n- customer disclosures; and\n- whether any deposit-insurance
      statement is accurate for the actual account structure.\n\n**Recommended closing
      position**\n\nKeep the acquisition closing separate from the regulated-service
      launch.\n\nFor the four Harbor-only states, use a state launch hold unless Mosaic
      has documented authority. For overlapping states, confirm that Mosaic’s existing
      licenses cover the complete post-close product set and funds flow.\n\nDo not
      surrender Harbor’s licenses or release its bonds until the applicable regulators
      permit it and all outstanding obligations have been assigned, completed, or
      placed into an approved run-off structure.\n\nThis investigation does not change
      the current recommendation. It adds one material point: **partner-bank custody
      is part of the licensing analysis, but custody alone does not cure an authority
      gap.**\n\n</details>\n\nResearch history: [Saved research 1](03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/RES-20260911-0a5f07.md)\n\n</details>\n<!--
      retained-issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-94e42e2b38090045:end -->\n\n<!-- issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-ca95e529feeb1045
      -->\n### BSA/AML and KYC: whether relying on Harbor's prior KYC (with missing
      underlying documents for pre-2023 users) satisfies Mosaic Relay's independent
      CIP/AML obligations\n\n**Current answer**\n\nThe current no-reverification plan
      is not yet supported. Mosaic’s compliance team has not confirmed that its AML
      and customer identification program permits reliance on Harbor’s prior KYC results.\n\nThe
      missing documents prove a record gap. They do not, by themselves, prove that
      identification was not completed or that a violation occurred.\n\n**Rule and
      support**\n\nThe applicable rule depends on Mosaic’s legal status, its AML program,
      the permitted form of reliance, and the records that must remain available.
      This issue has not been researched. No saved authority establishes that Harbor’s
      pass/fail results are sufficient or insufficient.\n\n**Application**\n\nHarbor’s
      KYC vendor closed in 2024. Mosaic has pass/fail results for all users but lacks
      underlying documents for users onboarded before 2023. Mosaic plans to migrate
      the users without re-verification.\n\nWhether that is supportable depends on
      what Harbor collected, which records remain available, whether Mosaic can lawfully
      rely on Harbor’s process, and what Mosaic’s own program requires. The saved
      record does not answer these points.\n\n**Next step**\n\nCompliance should compare
      the available Harbor records with Mosaic’s program requirements. The evidence
      needed is:\n\n- Harbor’s KYC procedures and historical requirements;\n- available
      customer data and verification results;\n- the affected user count;\n- record-retention
      and access terms;\n- Mosaic’s reliance and remediation rules; and\n- a documented
      plan for users whose records are insufficient.\n\nUntil then, Mosaic should
      consider restrictions or staged access for affected users rather than assume
      that all prior KYC is sufficient.\n\n<!-- retained-issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-ca95e529feeb1045:start
      -->\nInitial analysis — no completed research is saved for this issue.\n<!--
      retained-issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-ca95e529feeb1045:end -->\n\n<!-- issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-69af3f282d21ef38
      -->\n### SAR and alert handling: obligations to review, file, and manage Harbor's
      ~40 open alerts and SARs, and successor liability\n\n**Current answer**\n\nThe
      alert and SAR review is a pre-close diligence priority. The saved record does
      not support a conclusion about filing duties, inherited liability, or whether
      any customer presents a material risk because nobody has reviewed the relevant
      history.\n\n**Rule and support**\n\nThe responsible entity’s duties depend on
      the activity dates, transaction history, filing deadlines, deal terms, post-close
      operating roles, and applicable AML rules. This issue has not been researched.\n\nAccess
      to SAR information can be legally restricted. Diligence must distinguish permitted
      review from disclosure or transfer of protected material.\n\n**Application**\n\nDiligence
      found approximately 40 open alerts and a handful of SARs from the prior year.
      They remain unread. This creates an information gap about unresolved suspicious
      activity, overdue review, affected accounts, and any needed post-close controls.\n\nThe
      asset purchase does not, on the saved material, answer which entity must finish
      each alert or make any required filing.\n\n**Next step**\n\nArrange a legally
      permitted review before migration. Create a case list showing:\n\n- alert age
      and status;\n- affected account;\n- transactions involved;\n- required decision
      or escalation;\n- filing status and deadline, if any;\n- responsible pre-close
      and post-close entity; and\n- any required account restriction.\n\nEscalate
      material findings into the closing and migration conditions.\n\n<!-- retained-issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-69af3f282d21ef38:start
      -->\nInitial analysis — no completed research is saved for this issue.\n<!--
      retained-issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-69af3f282d21ef38:end -->\n\n<!-- issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-4825f0af7ec75542
      -->\n### Monitoring vendor transition: continuity of transaction monitoring
      when the SaaS contract terminates at close\n\n**Current answer**\n\nThe monitoring
      transition should be tested and accepted before migrated users receive full
      product access. The saved assumption that repointing will work without a monitoring
      gap remains open.\n\n**Rule and support**\n\nIf Mosaic’s applicable AML program
      requires transaction monitoring, its implemented controls must cover the activities
      and users that move to Mosaic. The exact requirements and any permitted transition
      measures have not been researched.\n\n**Application**\n\nHarbor’s monitoring
      software contract ends at close. Mosaic plans to repoint Harbor’s transaction
      feed to Mosaic’s engine on day one. The current record contains no test results,
      data mapping, alert validation, capacity review, or fallback plan.\n\nA technical
      connection alone would not show that Mosaic’s rules detect the relevant Harbor
      activity or that no transactions are lost during the change.\n\n**Next step**\n\nComplete
      an end-to-end test before migration. Evidence should include:\n\n- all expected
      transaction types and fields;\n- successful data reconciliation;\n- rule coverage
      for Harbor products;\n- test alerts and case creation;\n- operating ownership;\n-
      cutover and rollback procedures; and\n- monitoring coverage during any outage.\n\nIf
      the test does not pass, continue the existing service if possible or limit the
      affected transactions until monitoring is effective.\n\n<!-- retained-issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-4825f0af7ec75542:start
      -->\nInitial analysis — no completed research is saved for this issue.\n<!--
      retained-issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-4825f0af7ec75542:end -->\n\n<!-- issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-72810d0a43d3ce66
      -->\n### Marketing compliance: the $50 welcome bonus offer and any state money
      transmission or consumer protection implications\n\n**Current answer**\n\nThe
      welcome bonus needs review before release, but it is lower priority than licensing
      and AML readiness. The saved record does not contain enough information to determine
      the required terms or disclosures.\n\n**Rule and support**\n\nThe applicable
      requirements depend on how customers earn the bonus, who funds and pays it,
      any transfer or holding conditions, expiration terms, geographic limits, and
      the accuracy of the advertising. This issue has not been researched.\n\n**Application**\n\nMarketing
      wants to launch a $50 bonus on the first transfer at close+1. The structure
      remains unknown. It is not established whether the bonus is deposited into the
      account, applied as a credit, subject to repayment or forfeiture, or limited
      to particular customers or states.\n\nThe campaign also depends on Mosaic having
      authority to offer the underlying service in each market.\n\n**Next step**\n\nObtain
      the final offer terms, customer journey, funding flow, advertising copy, eligibility
      rules, exclusions, expiration terms, and state coverage. Review those materials
      before publication. Do not advertise service in a state where Mosaic’s operating
      authority remains unresolved.\n\n<!-- retained-issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-72810d0a43d3ce66:start
      -->\nInitial analysis — no completed research is saved for this issue.\n<!--
      retained-issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-72810d0a43d3ce66:end -->\n\n## Alternatives considered\n\n<!--
      alternative:SCN-0f23edfc89ec6ecaf60a13e4 -->\n### Bank-held funds timing test
      — **Hypothetical — not adopted**\n\n[Saved alternative](03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/scenarios/SCN-0f23edfc89ec6ecaf60a13e4.md)\n\n**Assumptions**\n\nFor
      this test only:\n\n- A bank would hold the relevant funds.\n- Mosaic would control
      transfer instructions.\n- The structure depends on completing the bank contract
      and operating setup.\n- Migration would occur on November 15, 2026.\n- Bank
      integration would be ready on December 3, 2026.\n- Pre-2023 identity documents
      would remain missing.\n- Forty alert cases would remain pending.\n- The monitoring
      service would end at close.\n\nThese dates and conditions are test facts only.
      They do not change the actual close record or current direction.\n\n**Benefits**\n\nBank
      custody could support a different operating structure if the bank is the actual
      provider and the contracts, customer terms, control rights, and operations support
      that position. The saved test does not establish that result.\n\n**Trade-offs
      and added risks**\n\nOn the test dates, bank integration would not be ready
      until 18 days after the planned November 15 migration. This makes the alternative
      harder to use for that migration.\n\nMosaic would still control transfer instructions.
      The saved material does not show that bank custody alone changes Mosaic’s licensing
      position. Failure to complete the bank contract or operating setup in time would
      prevent use of this option on the test date.\n\n**Issues that remain**\n\nThe
      alternative does not resolve:\n\n- missing pre-2023 identity documents;\n- 40
      pending alert cases;\n- monitoring ending at close;\n- Mosaic’s authority for
      its actual role;\n- customer contract terms; or\n- allocation of AML and reporting
      duties.\n\n**Next action**\n\nProposed, not assigned: Mara Chen should obtain
      a dated bank-readiness plan and confirm whether the bank can support November
      15, 2026. The plan should also assign work for the identity records, pending
      alerts, and replacement monitoring.\n\n<!-- alternative:SCN-406906c91ab0aba4313b54a6
      -->\n### Bank-held funds structure — **Hypothetical — not adopted**\n\n[Saved
      alternative](03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/scenarios/SCN-406906c91ab0aba4313b54a6.md)\n\n**Assumptions**\n\nFor
      this test only, a partner bank would hold customer funds. The recorded acquisition
      remains an asset purchase.\n\n**Benefits**\n\nA bank-led model could produce
      a different money-transmission analysis if the bank were the actual provider
      and Mosaic performed only permitted services. The saved alternative does not
      establish these facts.\n\n**Trade-offs and added risks**\n\nThe structure would
      require contracts and operations that match the claimed bank role. It could
      add dependencies for bank consent, account setup, operating control, reconciliation,
      customer terms, and service continuity.\n\nNo saved working note or test evidence
      establishes readiness or a timing benefit.\n\n**Issues that remain**\n\nThe
      following points are unresolved:\n\n- who contracts directly with users;\n-
      who controls transfer instructions and settlement;\n- who records balances and
      owes repayment;\n- how KYC and monitoring duties are allocated;\n- who handles
      alerts and SARs;\n- how privacy duties are allocated; and\n- who is responsible
      for the welcome bonus.\n\nHolding funds at a bank does not, on the saved material,
      resolve these issues.\n\n**Next action**\n\nProposed, not assigned: prepare
      a funds-flow and responsibility map. Obtain the bank agreements, customer terms,
      account titles, control rights, settlement process, loss allocation, and bank
      consent requirements before comparing this structure with the current plan.\n\n##
      Next actions\n\n1. **Complete the four-state licensing matrix.**  \n   **Status:**
      Proposed; no assignment recorded.  \n   **Proposed owners:** Licensing team
      and state regulatory counsel.  \n   **Date:** Before the targeted Nov 15 close;
      no separate due date or year is recorded for this action.  \n   **Evidence needed:**
      Applicable state rules, filing deadlines, regulator responses, Mosaic and Harbor
      license status, post-close funds flow, and any interim authority.\n\n2. **Review
      Harbor’s open alerts and SAR history through a permitted diligence process.**
      \ \n   **Status:** Proposed; no assignment recorded.  \n   **Owner:** No owner
      recorded.  \n   **Date:** Before migration; no due date recorded.  \n   **Evidence
      needed:** Case inventory, age, status, filing history, responsible entity, deadlines,
      and account-control decisions.\n\n3. **Decide whether Mosaic’s AML and customer
      identification program permits reliance on Harbor’s KYC.**  \n   **Status:**
      Proposed; no assignment recorded.  \n   **Owner:** No owner recorded; compliance
      confirmation is required by the saved facts.  \n   **Date:** Before migration;
      no due date recorded.  \n   **Evidence needed:** Mosaic’s program requirements,
      Harbor’s procedures, available customer records, affected user count, and remediation
      criteria.\n\n4. **Test the monitoring cutover.**  \n   **Status:** Proposed;
      no assignment recorded.  \n   **Owner:** No owner recorded.  \n   **Date:**
      Before migration; no due date recorded.  \n   **Evidence needed:** Reconciliation
      results, rule coverage, test alerts, ownership, outage coverage, and rollback
      plan.\n\n5. **Review the welcome-bonus structure and advertising.**  \n   **Status:**
      Proposed; no assignment recorded.  \n   **Owner:** No owner recorded.  \n   **Date:**
      Before the planned close+1 launch; no due date recorded.  \n   **Evidence needed:**
      Final terms, funding flow, eligibility, restrictions, customer disclosures,
      advertising copy, and state coverage.\n\n## Current work product and supporting
      records\n\n- Current work product: No current draft or final path is saved.\n-
      [Latest licensing research](03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/RES-20260911-0a5f07.md)\n-
      [Known facts](03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/facts.md)\n- [Original request](03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/request.md)\n-
      [Prior dossier revision](03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/dossier-revisions/DOS-c993ab2326efd727cc3d.md)\n-
      [Bank-held funds timing test](03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/scenarios/SCN-0f23edfc89ec6ecaf60a13e4.md)\n-
      [Bank-held funds structure](03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/scenarios/SCN-406906c91ab0aba4313b54a6.md)\n\n[Open
      saved dossier revision](03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/dossier-revisions/DOS-6e1b2d11381df87e0eab.md)\n\nDossier
      generated and saved."
    record_revision: 833a40f5ea549e09788a8cf3b56aa8dc4ec045a84ad91b16b7d73903807ce832
    source_version: 833a40f5ea549e09788a8cf3b56aa8dc4ec045a84ad91b16b7d73903807ce832
    support_state: supplied
    captured_record:
      message_id: MSG-20260912-7d0b75
      content: "# Harbor 2 matter dossier\n\n## Current position\n\nThe Harbor Pay
        migration needs legal and compliance review before the targeted Nov 15 close.
        This is the accepted working view, not a recorded decision.\n\nMosaic plans
        to buy Harbor’s assets and move 240,000 consumer users and 12,000 business
        accounts into one Mosaic account on day one. The main obstacles are:\n\n1.
        Mosaic has not shown that it can use Harbor’s licenses in the four Harbor-only
        states.\n2. Mosaic has not confirmed that Harbor’s prior know-your-customer
        (KYC) results satisfy Mosaic’s anti-money-laundering (AML) program.\n3. Harbor’s
        approximately 40 open alerts and recent suspicious activity report (SAR) history
        remain unread.\n4. The current monitoring service ends at close, and the replacement
        feed has not been shown to work.\n5. The terms of the $50 welcome bonus are
        not known.\n\nThe current direction remains unchanged. A full day-one migration
        is the planning baseline, but the saved working view supports launch restrictions
        where these conditions are not resolved. No decisions are recorded.\n\nThe
        analysis assumes that Harbor and Mosaic conduct regulated money-transmission
        activity and that Mosaic will become the operating provider immediately after
        close. It does not assume that Harbor’s licenses transfer, that Harbor’s KYC
        records are sufficient, or that the monitoring change will work without a
        gap.\n\nA newer reported answer confirms that the deal is an asset purchase.
        It replaces the earlier assumption that the deal structure was unknown.\n\n##
        Decision question\n\nDoes any part of the Harbor Pay acquisition migration
        need legal review before the Nov 15 close?\n\n## Issues\n\n<!-- issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-94e42e2b38090045
        -->\n### Money transmission licensing: whether Mosaic Relay can operate under
        the combined 44-state footprint and whether Harbor's MTLs transfer/survive
        the acquisition\n\n**Current answer**\n\nMosaic should not rely on Harbor’s
        money transmitter licenses as assets that automatically move to Mosaic. Before
        Mosaic starts regulated services in a state, it needs documented authority
        for the complete post-close activity in that state.\n\nThe asset purchase
        may close separately from the service launch. For the four Harbor-only states,
        the current working position is to hold regulated functions unless Mosaic
        has its own license, required state approval, a supported interim structure,
        or another documented basis to operate.\n\n**Rule and support**\n\nThe saved
        research concludes that the answer must be determined state by state. The
        review must cover Mosaic’s authority, Harbor’s notices or approvals, outstanding
        obligations, interim operation, and license surrender.\n\nFour sources were
        retrieved, but no relevant passages were read or verified. The saved materials
        therefore do not establish an operative state rule:\n\n- [Research packet](03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/RES-20260911-0a5f07.md)\n-
        [CSBS Money Transmission Modernization Act — retrieved, passage not reviewed](03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/sources/SRC-37ee676da5b354ba8538-157583424427.md)\n-
        [New York Department of Financial Services licensing page — retrieved, passage
        not reviewed](03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/sources/SRC-71b20ecf65825d6522c4-fa80c95c5393.md)\n\nThe
        model act is not proof of the law in any specific state.\n\n**Application**\n\nHarbor
        reportedly holds licenses in 38 states. Mosaic reportedly holds licenses in
        41 states. The proposed combined footprint has 44 states and would open four
        new markets. Mosaic currently plans to use or assume Harbor’s licenses in
        those markets. The deal is an asset purchase.\n\nThe unified Mosaic account,
        combined balance, and access to Mosaic products suggest that Mosaic may become
        the actual provider. If so, keeping Harbor’s entity or licenses active might
        not give Mosaic authority. A transition in which Harbor remains the licensed
        provider could be possible only if the contracts, funds flow, customer terms,
        operating control, and applicable state requirements support that result.\n\nBank
        custody alone would not establish that Mosaic can perform the regulated activity.
        The full allocation of customer obligations and transfer control remains material.\n\n**Next
        step**\n\nIdentify the four Harbor-only states. Prepare one state matrix covering:\n\n-
        Mosaic’s authority for each post-close activity;\n- required transaction notices
        and approvals;\n- whether interim operation is permitted;\n- outstanding customer
        obligations;\n- bank custody and control;\n- Harbor’s run-off duties; and\n-
        license surrender requirements.\n\nDo not activate regulated functions in
        a state without documented authority.\n\n<!-- retained-issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-94e42e2b38090045:start
        -->\n<details>\n<summary>Saved research and prior analysis</summary>\n\nDo
        not rely on transferability without an applicable state rule, approval, or
        written regulator position.\n\nThe answer must be determined state by state
        and must cover more than technical change-of-control rules.\n\nThis path is
        possible only if Harbor remains the real provider and the relevant states
        permit the arrangement. The unified Mosaic account design may conflict with
        that position.\n\nThe result depends on the contracts and actual funds flow.
        Custody alone should not be treated as a licensing exemption or transfer mechanism.\n\nResearch
        saved. 4 sources retrieved; 0 with passages read.\n\nNext step: Identify the
        four Harbor-only states and complete one matrix covering license authority,
        transaction filings, interim operation, bank custody, outstanding obligations,
        and license surrender.\n\n<details>\n<summary>Detailed analysis</summary>\n\n**Practical
        answer**\n\n**Do not treat Harbor’s money transmitter licenses as assets that
        move to Mosaic in the asset purchase.** Use this closing rule:\n\n> **No confirmed
        authority means no regulated launch by Mosaic in that state.**\n\nThe asset
        purchase can still close. However, Mosaic should not activate regulated services
        in the four Harbor-only states unless one of these conditions is met:\n\n1.
        Mosaic obtains its own license.\n2. The state approves a transfer or other
        license change.\n3. The regulator confirms a lawful interim structure.\n4.
        Harbor remains the real licensed provider under a valid transition plan.\n5.
        Mosaic blocks regulated functions in that state.\n\nThe planned unified Mosaic
        account is important. It suggests that Mosaic, not Harbor, will become the
        service provider. If that is correct, keeping Harbor’s legal entity and licenses
        alive might not give Mosaic authority to operate.\n\n**No verified operative
        authority retrieved.** The collection found the CSBS Model Money Transmission
        Modernization Act and a New York regulator licensing page. However, no exact
        provision about an asset sale, license transfer, interim operation, or custody
        was available for review. Also, a model act is not proof of the law adopted
        in a specific state. The state-specific conclusions below remain conditional.\n\n**Work
        needed before launch**\n\n| Work | Why | Proposed owner | Needed by | Evidence
        to proceed | Fallback |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| Identify the four Harbor-only
        states | These states have the clearest authority gap | Licensing team | Now
        | Confirmed license list and covered activities | Block regulated functions
        in those states |\n| Build a matrix for every affected state | Transfer, notice,
        approval, and surrender rules can differ | State regulatory counsel | Before
        the closing conditions are final | Applicable rule, filing deadline, regulator
        response, and license status | Delay migration in the affected state |\n|
        Confirm the post-close service provider | The licensed entity must match the
        actual operating model | Product and deal counsel | Before customer notices
        and account design are final | Contracts, user interface, funds-flow diagram,
        and operating roles | Keep Harbor as the actual provider where permitted |\n|
        Review notices and approvals | An asset sale can create separate seller and
        buyer requirements | Licensing team | Before each state deadline | Filing
        receipts and required approvals or non-objections | Make approval a state
        launch condition |\n| Confirm interim operating authority | A pending application
        should not be assumed to permit operation | State regulatory counsel | Before
        close | Applicable rule or written regulator confirmation | Keep Harbor customers
        segregated or restrict transactions |\n| Review partner-bank arrangements
        | Bank custody does not, by itself, establish Mosaic’s licensing position
        | Payments counsel and bank relationship owner | Before balances move | Bank
        agreements, account titles, control rights, reconciliation design, and bank
        consent | Keep funds and obligations in the existing structure |\n| Prepare
        license run-off and surrender plans | Harbor may retain duties for balances,
        claims, records, and reports | Harbor compliance and deal counsel | Before
        surrender or operational shutdown | Regulator-approved plan and continued
        bonds and financial coverage | Keep Harbor licensed and operational longer
        |\n\n**State notice and approval**\n\nDo not search only for a rule called
        “license transfer.” Review each state for:\n\n- sale of substantially all
        assets;\n- change of control;\n- change in business plan;\n- change in licensed
        activities;\n- assignment of outstanding payment obligations;\n- change in
        responsible persons, locations, or agents;\n- license surrender or termination;\n-
        customer agreement assignment; and\n- movement of stored balances or customer
        funds.\n\nAn asset purchase might fall outside a technical “change of control”
        rule because Harbor’s ownership does not change. That does not resolve the
        issue. Harbor might still need a notice or surrender approval. Mosaic might
        separately need authority before it starts the regulated activity.\n\nThe
        state matrix should distinguish:\n\n- notice only;\n- approval before closing;\n-
        approval before customer migration;\n- new license required;\n- operation
        permitted while an application is pending;\n- regulator-approved transition
        available; and\n- no supported interim path.\n\n**Interim operation**\n\nHarbor
        could remain the licensed provider for a transition period. However, the structure
        must be real, not only contractual language.\n\nA supportable structure would
        normally need to show that:\n\n- Harbor remains responsible to customers.\n-
        Customer terms name Harbor as the provider.\n- Harbor controls the regulated
        activity as required.\n- Harbor’s licenses, bonds, and financial coverage
        remain active.\n- Customer funds and outstanding obligations remain covered.\n-
        Harbor can complete reports, refunds, complaints, and examinations.\n- Required
        records remain available.\n- Mosaic’s operational role is permitted.\n- The
        partner bank accepts the structure.\n- Each relevant regulator permits or
        accepts the structure.\n\nThe present plan creates tension with this approach.
        A single Mosaic account, one combined balance, and full access to Mosaic products
        make it harder to show that Harbor remains the true provider.\n\n**Partner-bank
        custody**\n\nA partner bank account is not automatically a substitute for
        a money transmitter license. The key questions concern the complete activity
        and obligation flow.\n\nFor each customer balance and transfer, document:\n\n1.
        Who receives the customer’s instruction?\n2. Who accepts the obligation to
        transmit or return the funds?\n3. In whose name is the bank account held?\n4.
        Is it a custodial or “for benefit of” account?\n5. Who can direct withdrawals?\n6.
        Who owns the ledger and resolves errors?\n7. Who bears loss if a transfer
        fails?\n8. Which entity owes the customer a refund?\n9. Which entity’s license,
        bond, and permissible investments cover the obligation?\n10. Does the bank
        agreement permit assignment, migration, or a change in the program manager?\n11.
        Is bank consent required before closing or migration?\n12. What happens to
        funds if Harbor, Mosaic, or the bank relationship ends?\n\nA bank-led model
        might provide a different licensing analysis if the bank is the actual provider
        and Mosaic performs only permitted services. A bank account that merely holds
        funds for Mosaic or Harbor is different. The legal documents, customer terms,
        control rights, and actual operations must all support the claimed model.\n\nAlso
        review:\n\n- account segregation;\n- daily reconciliation;\n- shortfalls and
        reserve funding;\n- bank setoff rights;\n- liens and security interests;\n-
        continuity if the account is frozen;\n- treatment of pending transfers;\n-
        return and chargeback duties;\n- insolvency treatment;\n- customer disclosures;
        and\n- whether any deposit-insurance statement is accurate for the actual
        account structure.\n\n**Recommended closing position**\n\nKeep the acquisition
        closing separate from the regulated-service launch.\n\nFor the four Harbor-only
        states, use a state launch hold unless Mosaic has documented authority. For
        overlapping states, confirm that Mosaic’s existing licenses cover the complete
        post-close product set and funds flow.\n\nDo not surrender Harbor’s licenses
        or release its bonds until the applicable regulators permit it and all outstanding
        obligations have been assigned, completed, or placed into an approved run-off
        structure.\n\nThis investigation does not change the current recommendation.
        It adds one material point: **partner-bank custody is part of the licensing
        analysis, but custody alone does not cure an authority gap.**\n\n</details>\n\nResearch
        history: [Saved research 1](03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/RES-20260911-0a5f07.md)\n\n</details>\n<!--
        retained-issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-94e42e2b38090045:end -->\n\n<!-- issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-ca95e529feeb1045
        -->\n### BSA/AML and KYC: whether relying on Harbor's prior KYC (with missing
        underlying documents for pre-2023 users) satisfies Mosaic Relay's independent
        CIP/AML obligations\n\n**Current answer**\n\nThe current no-reverification
        plan is not yet supported. Mosaic’s compliance team has not confirmed that
        its AML and customer identification program permits reliance on Harbor’s prior
        KYC results.\n\nThe missing documents prove a record gap. They do not, by
        themselves, prove that identification was not completed or that a violation
        occurred.\n\n**Rule and support**\n\nThe applicable rule depends on Mosaic’s
        legal status, its AML program, the permitted form of reliance, and the records
        that must remain available. This issue has not been researched. No saved authority
        establishes that Harbor’s pass/fail results are sufficient or insufficient.\n\n**Application**\n\nHarbor’s
        KYC vendor closed in 2024. Mosaic has pass/fail results for all users but
        lacks underlying documents for users onboarded before 2023. Mosaic plans to
        migrate the users without re-verification.\n\nWhether that is supportable
        depends on what Harbor collected, which records remain available, whether
        Mosaic can lawfully rely on Harbor’s process, and what Mosaic’s own program
        requires. The saved record does not answer these points.\n\n**Next step**\n\nCompliance
        should compare the available Harbor records with Mosaic’s program requirements.
        The evidence needed is:\n\n- Harbor’s KYC procedures and historical requirements;\n-
        available customer data and verification results;\n- the affected user count;\n-
        record-retention and access terms;\n- Mosaic’s reliance and remediation rules;
        and\n- a documented plan for users whose records are insufficient.\n\nUntil
        then, Mosaic should consider restrictions or staged access for affected users
        rather than assume that all prior KYC is sufficient.\n\n<!-- retained-issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-ca95e529feeb1045:start
        -->\nInitial analysis — no completed research is saved for this issue.\n<!--
        retained-issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-ca95e529feeb1045:end -->\n\n<!-- issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-69af3f282d21ef38
        -->\n### SAR and alert handling: obligations to review, file, and manage Harbor's
        ~40 open alerts and SARs, and successor liability\n\n**Current answer**\n\nThe
        alert and SAR review is a pre-close diligence priority. The saved record does
        not support a conclusion about filing duties, inherited liability, or whether
        any customer presents a material risk because nobody has reviewed the relevant
        history.\n\n**Rule and support**\n\nThe responsible entity’s duties depend
        on the activity dates, transaction history, filing deadlines, deal terms,
        post-close operating roles, and applicable AML rules. This issue has not been
        researched.\n\nAccess to SAR information can be legally restricted. Diligence
        must distinguish permitted review from disclosure or transfer of protected
        material.\n\n**Application**\n\nDiligence found approximately 40 open alerts
        and a handful of SARs from the prior year. They remain unread. This creates
        an information gap about unresolved suspicious activity, overdue review, affected
        accounts, and any needed post-close controls.\n\nThe asset purchase does not,
        on the saved material, answer which entity must finish each alert or make
        any required filing.\n\n**Next step**\n\nArrange a legally permitted review
        before migration. Create a case list showing:\n\n- alert age and status;\n-
        affected account;\n- transactions involved;\n- required decision or escalation;\n-
        filing status and deadline, if any;\n- responsible pre-close and post-close
        entity; and\n- any required account restriction.\n\nEscalate material findings
        into the closing and migration conditions.\n\n<!-- retained-issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-69af3f282d21ef38:start
        -->\nInitial analysis — no completed research is saved for this issue.\n<!--
        retained-issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-69af3f282d21ef38:end -->\n\n<!-- issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-4825f0af7ec75542
        -->\n### Monitoring vendor transition: continuity of transaction monitoring
        when the SaaS contract terminates at close\n\n**Current answer**\n\nThe monitoring
        transition should be tested and accepted before migrated users receive full
        product access. The saved assumption that repointing will work without a monitoring
        gap remains open.\n\n**Rule and support**\n\nIf Mosaic’s applicable AML program
        requires transaction monitoring, its implemented controls must cover the activities
        and users that move to Mosaic. The exact requirements and any permitted transition
        measures have not been researched.\n\n**Application**\n\nHarbor’s monitoring
        software contract ends at close. Mosaic plans to repoint Harbor’s transaction
        feed to Mosaic’s engine on day one. The current record contains no test results,
        data mapping, alert validation, capacity review, or fallback plan.\n\nA technical
        connection alone would not show that Mosaic’s rules detect the relevant Harbor
        activity or that no transactions are lost during the change.\n\n**Next step**\n\nComplete
        an end-to-end test before migration. Evidence should include:\n\n- all expected
        transaction types and fields;\n- successful data reconciliation;\n- rule coverage
        for Harbor products;\n- test alerts and case creation;\n- operating ownership;\n-
        cutover and rollback procedures; and\n- monitoring coverage during any outage.\n\nIf
        the test does not pass, continue the existing service if possible or limit
        the affected transactions until monitoring is effective.\n\n<!-- retained-issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-4825f0af7ec75542:start
        -->\nInitial analysis — no completed research is saved for this issue.\n<!--
        retained-issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-4825f0af7ec75542:end -->\n\n<!-- issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-72810d0a43d3ce66
        -->\n### Marketing compliance: the $50 welcome bonus offer and any state money
        transmission or consumer protection implications\n\n**Current answer**\n\nThe
        welcome bonus needs review before release, but it is lower priority than licensing
        and AML readiness. The saved record does not contain enough information to
        determine the required terms or disclosures.\n\n**Rule and support**\n\nThe
        applicable requirements depend on how customers earn the bonus, who funds
        and pays it, any transfer or holding conditions, expiration terms, geographic
        limits, and the accuracy of the advertising. This issue has not been researched.\n\n**Application**\n\nMarketing
        wants to launch a $50 bonus on the first transfer at close+1. The structure
        remains unknown. It is not established whether the bonus is deposited into
        the account, applied as a credit, subject to repayment or forfeiture, or limited
        to particular customers or states.\n\nThe campaign also depends on Mosaic
        having authority to offer the underlying service in each market.\n\n**Next
        step**\n\nObtain the final offer terms, customer journey, funding flow, advertising
        copy, eligibility rules, exclusions, expiration terms, and state coverage.
        Review those materials before publication. Do not advertise service in a state
        where Mosaic’s operating authority remains unresolved.\n\n<!-- retained-issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-72810d0a43d3ce66:start
        -->\nInitial analysis — no completed research is saved for this issue.\n<!--
        retained-issue:ISS-c9d7c39f63-72810d0a43d3ce66:end -->\n\n## Alternatives
        considered\n\n<!-- alternative:SCN-0f23edfc89ec6ecaf60a13e4 -->\n### Bank-held
        funds timing test — **Hypothetical — not adopted**\n\n[Saved alternative](03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/scenarios/SCN-0f23edfc89ec6ecaf60a13e4.md)\n\n**Assumptions**\n\nFor
        this test only:\n\n- A bank would hold the relevant funds.\n- Mosaic would
        control transfer instructions.\n- The structure depends on completing the
        bank contract and operating setup.\n- Migration would occur on November 15,
        2026.\n- Bank integration would be ready on December 3, 2026.\n- Pre-2023
        identity documents would remain missing.\n- Forty alert cases would remain
        pending.\n- The monitoring service would end at close.\n\nThese dates and
        conditions are test facts only. They do not change the actual close record
        or current direction.\n\n**Benefits**\n\nBank custody could support a different
        operating structure if the bank is the actual provider and the contracts,
        customer terms, control rights, and operations support that position. The
        saved test does not establish that result.\n\n**Trade-offs and added risks**\n\nOn
        the test dates, bank integration would not be ready until 18 days after the
        planned November 15 migration. This makes the alternative harder to use for
        that migration.\n\nMosaic would still control transfer instructions. The saved
        material does not show that bank custody alone changes Mosaic’s licensing
        position. Failure to complete the bank contract or operating setup in time
        would prevent use of this option on the test date.\n\n**Issues that remain**\n\nThe
        alternative does not resolve:\n\n- missing pre-2023 identity documents;\n-
        40 pending alert cases;\n- monitoring ending at close;\n- Mosaic’s authority
        for its actual role;\n- customer contract terms; or\n- allocation of AML and
        reporting duties.\n\n**Next action**\n\nProposed, not assigned: Mara Chen
        should obtain a dated bank-readiness plan and confirm whether the bank can
        support November 15, 2026. The plan should also assign work for the identity
        records, pending alerts, and replacement monitoring.\n\n<!-- alternative:SCN-406906c91ab0aba4313b54a6
        -->\n### Bank-held funds structure — **Hypothetical — not adopted**\n\n[Saved
        alternative](03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/scenarios/SCN-406906c91ab0aba4313b54a6.md)\n\n**Assumptions**\n\nFor
        this test only, a partner bank would hold customer funds. The recorded acquisition
        remains an asset purchase.\n\n**Benefits**\n\nA bank-led model could produce
        a different money-transmission analysis if the bank were the actual provider
        and Mosaic performed only permitted services. The saved alternative does not
        establish these facts.\n\n**Trade-offs and added risks**\n\nThe structure
        would require contracts and operations that match the claimed bank role. It
        could add dependencies for bank consent, account setup, operating control,
        reconciliation, customer terms, and service continuity.\n\nNo saved working
        note or test evidence establishes readiness or a timing benefit.\n\n**Issues
        that remain**\n\nThe following points are unresolved:\n\n- who contracts directly
        with users;\n- who controls transfer instructions and settlement;\n- who records
        balances and owes repayment;\n- how KYC and monitoring duties are allocated;\n-
        who handles alerts and SARs;\n- how privacy duties are allocated; and\n- who
        is responsible for the welcome bonus.\n\nHolding funds at a bank does not,
        on the saved material, resolve these issues.\n\n**Next action**\n\nProposed,
        not assigned: prepare a funds-flow and responsibility map. Obtain the bank
        agreements, customer terms, account titles, control rights, settlement process,
        loss allocation, and bank consent requirements before comparing this structure
        with the current plan.\n\n## Next actions\n\n1. **Complete the four-state
        licensing matrix.**  \n   **Status:** Proposed; no assignment recorded.  \n
        \  **Proposed owners:** Licensing team and state regulatory counsel.  \n   **Date:**
        Before the targeted Nov 15 close; no separate due date or year is recorded
        for this action.  \n   **Evidence needed:** Applicable state rules, filing
        deadlines, regulator responses, Mosaic and Harbor license status, post-close
        funds flow, and any interim authority.\n\n2. **Review Harbor’s open alerts
        and SAR history through a permitted diligence process.**  \n   **Status:**
        Proposed; no assignment recorded.  \n   **Owner:** No owner recorded.  \n
        \  **Date:** Before migration; no due date recorded.  \n   **Evidence needed:**
        Case inventory, age, status, filing history, responsible entity, deadlines,
        and account-control decisions.\n\n3. **Decide whether Mosaic’s AML and customer
        identification program permits reliance on Harbor’s KYC.**  \n   **Status:**
        Proposed; no assignment recorded.  \n   **Owner:** No owner recorded; compliance
        confirmation is required by the saved facts.  \n   **Date:** Before migration;
        no due date recorded.  \n   **Evidence needed:** Mosaic’s program requirements,
        Harbor’s procedures, available customer records, affected user count, and
        remediation criteria.\n\n4. **Test the monitoring cutover.**  \n   **Status:**
        Proposed; no assignment recorded.  \n   **Owner:** No owner recorded.  \n
        \  **Date:** Before migration; no due date recorded.  \n   **Evidence needed:**
        Reconciliation results, rule coverage, test alerts, ownership, outage coverage,
        and rollback plan.\n\n5. **Review the welcome-bonus structure and advertising.**
        \ \n   **Status:** Proposed; no assignment recorded.  \n   **Owner:** No owner
        recorded.  \n   **Date:** Before the planned close+1 launch; no due date recorded.
        \ \n   **Evidence needed:** Final terms, funding flow, eligibility, restrictions,
        customer disclosures, advertising copy, and state coverage.\n\n## Current
        work product and supporting records\n\n- Current work product: No current
        draft or final path is saved.\n- [Latest licensing research](03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/RES-20260911-0a5f07.md)\n-
        [Known facts](03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/facts.md)\n- [Original request](03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/request.md)\n-
        [Prior dossier revision](03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/dossier-revisions/DOS-c993ab2326efd727cc3d.md)\n-
        [Bank-held funds timing test](03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/scenarios/SCN-0f23edfc89ec6ecaf60a13e4.md)\n-
        [Bank-held funds structure](03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/scenarios/SCN-406906c91ab0aba4313b54a6.md)\n\n[Open
        saved dossier revision](03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/dossier-revisions/DOS-6e1b2d11381df87e0eab.md)\n\nDossier
        generated and saved."

    source_class: saved_artifact
    path: 03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/facts.md
    available_excerpt: '# Known Facts


      - Mosaic Relay is acquiring Harbor Pay; close targeted Nov 15 (~10 weeks out).

      - Migration moves 240k consumer users and 12k business accounts into Mosaic
      Relay''s app on day 1 post-close, with one unified account and balance and full
      access to Mosaic''s product set.

      - Plan is no re-verification; Harbor users were already KYC''d by Harbor.

      - Harbor holds money transmitter licenses (MTLs) in 38 states; Mosaic Relay
      holds 41; combined footprint is 44 states, opening four new markets.

      - Harbor''s KYC ran through a vendor that shut down in 2024; Mosaic has pass/fail
      results for everyone but not the underlying documents for users onboarded before
      2023.

      - Harbor''s transaction monitoring is a third-party SaaS whose contract terminates
      at close; plan is to repoint Harbor''s transaction feed at Mosaic''s engine
      on day 1.

      - Marketing wants a ''Welcome'' campaign with a $50 bonus on first transfer,
      launching close+1.

      - Diligence flagged ~40 open alerts and ''a handful'' of SARs from the last
      year at Harbor; nobody has read them yet.

      - Migration moves 240k consumer users and 12k business accounts into Mosaic
      Relay''s app on day 1 post-close, with one unified account and balance.

      - Plan is no re-verification of Harbor''s existing KYC; Harbor users were already
      KYC''d.

      - Harbor''s KYC ran through a vendor that shut down in 2024; pass/fail results
      exist for everyone but underlying documents are missing for users onboarded
      before 2023.

      - Harbor''s transaction monitoring is a third-party SaaS whose contract terminates
      at close; plan is to repoint the transaction feed to Mosaic Relay''s engine
      on day 1.

      - Diligence flagged ~40 open alerts and ''a handful'' of SARs from the last
      year at Harbor; none have been read yet.

      - How is the Harbor Pay acquisition structured? — Asset purchase (Mosaic buys
      Harbor''s assets)

      - Does Mosaic Relay''s AML/CIP program currently permit relying on another institution''s
      prior KYC without re-verification, or is re-verification required? — Not sure
      / need to check with compliance

      - What is known about Harbor''s ~40 open alerts and the SARs from the last year?
      — Nothing reviewed yet — still in diligence

      - For the four new markets opened by the combined footprint, does Mosaic Relay
      plan to operate under Harbor''s existing licenses or apply for its own? — Use/assume
      Harbor''s licenses

      - For the pre-2023 Harbor users with no underlying KYC documents, what is the
      plan to satisfy Mosaic Relay''s own BSA/AML customer identification and recordkeeping
      obligations? — It is not yet known whether Mosaic Relay''s AML/CIP program permits
      reliance on Harbor''s prior KYC; compliance must confirm.

      - Have the ~40 open alerts and the handful of SARs from the last year been reviewed,
      and what did they reveal? — The open alerts and SAR history have not been reviewed
      and remain in diligence.

      - For the four new markets opened by the combined license footprint, what is
      the plan for state money transmission licensing or approval before onboarding
      those customers? — Mosaic Relay currently plans to use or assume Harbor''s licenses
      for the four new markets.


      ## Assumptions


      - [Assumption] Mosaic Relay''s existing money transmission licenses and Harbor''s
      MTLs are transferable or can be maintained through the acquisition without new
      state approval

      - [Assumption] The KYC pass/fail results are sufficient to satisfy Mosaic Relay''s
      own BSA/AML program obligations without the underlying documents

      - [Assumption] The transaction monitoring repoint can be completed and validated
      before close without a monitoring gap

      - [Assumption] Harbor Pay is a licensed money transmitter subject to state MTL
      regimes and federal BSA/AML obligations (not yet confirmed).

      - [Assumption] Mosaic Relay is itself a licensed money transmitter in 41 states
      (per the request).

      - [Assumption] The acquisition is structured as an asset or stock purchase,
      which affects whether Harbor''s licenses and SAR/alert obligations transfer
      (structure not yet confirmed).'

      content: '# Known Facts


        - Mosaic Relay is acquiring Harbor Pay; close targeted Nov 15 (~10 weeks out).

        - Migration moves 240k consumer users and 12k business accounts into Mosaic
        Relay''s app on day 1 post-close, with one unified account and balance and
        full access to Mosaic''s product set.

        - Plan is no re-verification; Harbor users were already KYC''d by Harbor.

        - Harbor holds money transmitter licenses (MTLs) in 38 states; Mosaic Relay
        holds 41; combined footprint is 44 states, opening four new markets.

        - Harbor''s KYC ran through a vendor that shut down in 2024; Mosaic has pass/fail
        results for everyone but not the underlying documents for users onboarded
        before 2023.

        - Harbor''s transaction monitoring is a third-party SaaS whose contract terminates
        at close; plan is to repoint Harbor''s transaction feed at Mosaic''s engine
        on day 1.

        - Marketing wants a ''Welcome'' campaign with a $50 bonus on first transfer,
        launching close+1.

        - Diligence flagged ~40 open alerts and ''a handful'' of SARs from the last
        year at Harbor; nobody has read them yet.

        - Migration moves 240k consumer users and 12k business accounts into Mosaic
        Relay''s app on day 1 post-close, with one unified account and balance.

        - Plan is no re-verification of Harbor''s existing KYC; Harbor users were
        already KYC''d.

        - Harbor''s KYC ran through a vendor that shut down in 2024; pass/fail results
        exist for everyone but underlying documents are missing for users onboarded
        before 2023.

        - Harbor''s transaction monitoring is a third-party SaaS whose contract terminates
        at close; plan is to repoint the transaction feed to Mosaic Relay''s engine
        on day 1.

        - Diligence flagged ~40 open alerts and ''a handful'' of SARs from the last
        year at Harbor; none have been read yet.

        - How is the Harbor Pay acquisition structured? — Asset purchase (Mosaic buys
        Harbor''s assets)

        - Does Mosaic Relay''s AML/CIP program currently permit relying on another
        institution''s prior KYC without re-verification, or is re-verification required?
        — Not sure / need to check with compliance

        - What is known about Harbor''s ~40 open alerts and the SARs from the last
        year? — Nothing reviewed yet — still in diligence

        - For the four new markets opened by the combined footprint, does Mosaic Relay
        plan to operate under Harbor''s existing licenses or apply for its own? —
        Use/assume Harbor''s licenses

        - For the pre-2023 Harbor users with no underlying KYC documents, what is
        the plan to satisfy Mosaic Relay''s own BSA/AML customer identification and

        - Have the ~40 open alerts and the handful of SARs from the last year been

        - For the four new markets opened by the combined license footprint, what
        is the plan for state money transmission licensing or approval before onboarding

        ## Assumptions


        - [Assumption] Mosaic Relay''s existing money transmission licenses and Harbor''s

        new state approval

        - [Assumption] The KYC pass/fail results are sufficient to satisfy Mosaic

        - [Assumption] The acquisition is structured as an asset or stock purchase,
        which affects whether Harbor''s licenses and SAR/alert obligations transfer

          source_action_key: workspace:workspace:696cd84283dc3a90b6fc1cbcf9d74a3be5d4cdb402afd6660f167ad60079b3f0:answer
        working_ask: Does the Harbor Pay acquisition migration require legal review
          before the Nov 15 close, and what legal workstreams must be addressed pre-close
          (money transmission licensing, KYC/BSA-AML, SAR/alerts handling, monitoring
          vendor transition, and the welcome bonus campaign)?
        issues:
        - 'Money transmission licensing: whether Mosaic Relay can operate under the
          combined 44-state footprint and whether Harbor''s MTLs transfer/survive
          the acquisition'
        - 'BSA/AML and KYC: whether relying on Harbor''s prior KYC (with missing underlying
          documents for pre-2023 users) satisfies Mosaic Relay''s independent CIP/AML
          obligations'
        - 'SAR and alert handling: obligations to review, file, and manage Harbor''s
          ~40 open alerts and SARs, and successor liability'
        - 'Monitoring vendor transition: continuity of transaction monitoring when
          the SaaS contract terminates at close'
        - 'Marketing compliance: the $50 welcome bonus offer and any state money transmission
          or consumer protection implications'
        open_questions:
        - Acquisition structure (asset vs. stock purchase) — determines whether Harbor's
          MTLs and regulatory obligations transfer to Mosaic Relay
        - Whether Harbor's state MTLs can be assumed/transferred or must be re-applied
          for in the four new markets
        - Whether Mosaic Relay's own AML program and CIP can lawfully rely on Harbor's
          prior KYC without re-verification
        - Details of the ~40 open alerts and SARs (nature, age, whether any are unresolved
          or reportable)
        - Whether the $50 welcome bonus is a deposit/interest-like offer implicating
          state money transmission or consumer protection rules
        public_research_questions:
        - State money transmitter license transfer/assumption requirements in an acquisition
          context
        - BSA/AML CIP obligations when relying on a prior financial institution's
          KYC in a merger/acquisition
        - Successor obligations for open SARs and alerts in an acquisition
        intake_answers:
        - answer_id: ANS-20260909-20ba3f
          question_id: Q-ACQ-STRUCTURE
          question: How is the Harbor Pay acquisition structured?
          answer: Asset purchase (Mosaic buys Harbor's assets)
          values:
          - asset_purchase
          status: answered
          record_target: fact
          source_id: MSG-20260909-2e549e
          source_action_key: experimental:a983e094-d247-45c1-955b-7cb3d672dbbf
          answered_at: '2026-09-09T20:56:00+00:00'
          answer_fact_id: FACT-20260909-ed9f06
        - answer_id: ANS-20260909-da570f
          question_id: Q-KYC-RELY
          question: Does Mosaic Relay's AML/CIP program currently permit relying on
            another institution's prior KYC without re-verification, or is re-verification
            required?
          answer: Not sure / need to check with compliance
          values:
          - unknown
          status: answered
          record_target: fact

    source_class: saved_artifact
    path: 03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/request.md
    available_excerpt: '# Original Request


      From: Dana, PM — Growth & Integration

      Subject: Harbor migration — anything here need legal?


      We''re acquiring Harbor Pay. Close is targeted Nov 15 (~10 weeks). Migration
      plan:


      240k consumer users, 12k business accounts move into our app on day 1 post-close.
      One unified account, one balance, full access to our product set.

      No re-verification — they were already KYC''d by Harbor.

      Harbor holds MTLs in 38 states, we hold 41. Combined that''s 44 — nice win,
      opens four new markets.

      Harbor''s KYC ran through a vendor that shut down in 2024. We have pass/fail
      results for everyone, but not the underlying documents for users onboarded before
      2023.

      Harbor''s monitoring is a third-party SaaS whose contract terminates at close.
      Plan is to repoint their transaction feed at our engine on day 1.

      Marketing wants a "Welcome" campaign with a $50 bonus on first transfer, launching
      close+1.

      Diligence flagged that Harbor has ~40 open alerts and "a handful" of SARs from
      the last year. Nobody''s read them yet.


      Does any of this need legal before close? It''s really just a data migration.'
